## [packages/core/src/application/returns.ts](../../packages/core/src/application/returns.ts)

IDs and predecessor belong to one immutable return attempt; refunds are positive XAF allocations.

```ts
export interface ReturnCommand {
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly originalId: string;
    readonly priorId?: string | undefined;
    readonly id: string;
    readonly reason: string;
    readonly items: readonly (ReturnSelection & {
        readonly id: string;
        readonly movementId: string;
    })[];
    readonly refunds: readonly {
        readonly id: string;
        readonly accountId: string;
        readonly amountMinor: number;
    }[];
    readonly debtTransactionId?: string | undefined;
}
```

Authorized immutable source/remaining quantities for a sale or purchase return; original prices are preserved.

```ts
returnableItems(context: OperationContext, kind: ReturnKind, originalId: string): ReturnSource;
```

Calculates exact discounted partial/full return value from current SQLite source facts and current predecessor.

```ts
previewReturn(context: OperationContext, kind: ReturnKind, originalId: string, items: readonly ReturnSelection[]): { totalMinor: number; costMinor: number; lines: readonly { originalItemId: string; productId: string; quantity: number; amountMinor: number; costMinor: number; }[]; } & { source: ReturnSource; };
```

Partial/full sale return under sales.refund: stock in, cash refund out (at most `refundableMinor`, what the sale collected less earlier refunds), remainder as customer credit; stale predecessor commits nothing.

```ts
returnSale(context: OperationContext, input: ReturnCommand): void;
```

Partial/full purchase return under purchases.create/pay: stock out, refund cash in (at most `refundableMinor`, what was paid at the door less earlier refunds), remainder reduces supplier debt; no original facts are edited.

```ts
returnPurchase(context: OperationContext, input: ReturnCommand): void;
```

## [packages/core/src/application/sealed-sync.ts](../../packages/core/src/application/sealed-sync.ts)

Native primitives for end-to-end encrypted sync (ADR-030): AES-256-GCM and codecs from the file-transfer
crypto, plus X25519 and HKDF-SHA256. Adapters use platform/OpenSSL implementations only.

```ts
export interface SealingCrypto extends TransferCrypto {
    ephemeralAgreement(recipientPublicKey: string): Promise<{
        readonly publicKey: string;
        readonly shared: string;
    }>;
    hkdf(ikmHex: string, saltHex: string, info: string, bytes: number): Promise<string>;
    hmac(keyHex: string, message: string): Promise<string>;
}
```

A shop key this device holds. `key` is 32 bytes of hex and only ever lives in memory.

```ts
export interface ShopKey {
    readonly keyId: string;
    readonly epoch: number;
    readonly key: string;
}
```

A 32-byte secret sealed to one device's X25519 key.

```ts
export type SealedKey = {
    readonly ephemeral_public_key: string;
    readonly nonce: string;
    readonly ciphertext: string;
    readonly tag: string;
};
```

Announces this device's X25519 key once, as a fact signed by this device, so the owner can seal shop keys to it.
Returns the public key, or undefined on a platform whose vault cannot hold one.

```ts
ensureEncryptionKey(context: OperationContext): Promise<string>;
```

Seals a hex secret to one device's X25519 key: ephemeral agreement, HKDF-SHA256, then AES-256-GCM bound to `aad`.

```ts
sealToDevice(crypto: SealingCrypto, recipientPublicKey: string, keyHex: string, aad: string): Promise<SealedKey>;
```

Opens a secret sealed to this installation's X25519 key; the private key stays in the vault.

```ts
openSealedWithVault(ports: WorkspacePorts, keyReference: string, ownPublicKey: string, sealed: SealedKey, aad: string, crypto: SealingCrypto): Promise<string>;
```

Every shop key granted to this device that it can open. A grant it cannot open is skipped, never fatal.

```ts
shopKeys(context: OperationContext, crypto: SealingCrypto): Promise<readonly ShopKey[]>;
```

The shop keys this device opens, and how many keys granted to it it could not open with `crypto` (none on a platform
without sealing). A device's encryption key never changes, so a missing key is a vault or storage failure; a backup
must not carry fewer keys than were granted (ADR-036). A shop that never used encrypted sync has no grants.

```ts
shopKeyCoverage(context: OperationContext, crypto: SealingCrypto | undefined): Promise<{ readonly keys: readonly ShopKey[]; readonly missing: number; }>;
```

The key new facts are sealed with: highest epoch, then highest key ID so two owner devices agree.

```ts
currentShopKey(keys: readonly ShopKey[]): ShopKey;
```

Owner key management, run in each sync pass (after pulling) on the owner's authority devices and a no-op elsewhere.
Creates the first shop key when the shop has none, opens a new epoch when a device that held the current key was revoked, and grants
every key this device holds to every trusted device that announced an encryption key. Returns grants added.

```ts
maintainShopKeys(context: OperationContext, crypto: SealingCrypto): Promise<number>;
```

Seals one signed fact with a shop key. The visible header is the AES-GCM additional data.

```ts
sealEvent(crypto: SealingCrypto, businessId: string, key: ShopKey, event: SignedJournalEvent): Promise<{ event_id: string; origin_device_id: string; origin_sequence: number; digest: string; key_id: string; epoch: number; nonce: string; ciphertext: string; tag: string; }>;
```

Opens a sealed fact, or returns undefined when this device lacks its key or the content does not match its header.
Origin proof is still checked afterwards, exactly as for a fact received by file.

```ts
openSealedEvent(crypto: SealingCrypto, businessId: string, keys: readonly ShopKey[], sealed: SealedEvent): Promise<{ envelope: { event_id: string; business_id: string; origin_device_id: string; actor_id: string; origin_sequence: number; event_type: string; event_version: number; occurred_at: string; authority_revision: number; required_permissions: ("products.view" | ... 41 more ... | "data.export")[]; payload: Record<.....;
```

Signed server batch of this device's pending facts for one server destination. Trust, encryption-key and
server-address facts travel clear; every other fact is sealed with the current shop key. Without a key only clear
facts are offered and `waiting` counts the facts held back. Offering never acknowledges delivery.

```ts
createSyncBatch(context: OperationContext, destinationId: string, crypto: SealingCrypto, keys: readonly ShopKey[], limit = 200): Promise<{ readonly signed?: { batch: { format: "kolo.sync-batch"; format_version: 1; batch_id: string; business_id: string; sender_device_id: string; receiver_device_id: string; created_at: string; events: ({ ...; } | { ...; })[]; }; digest: string; signature: string; }; readonly waiting: number; }>;
```

Authenticates a server-signed batch, opens the sealed facts this device has keys for and imports them with the
shared verification and application path. Facts it cannot open, or whose inner proof fails, are `locked`: nothing
is stored or acknowledged for them, so they are offered again later.

```ts
importSyncBatch(context: OperationContext, input: unknown, crypto: SealingCrypto, keys: readonly ShopKey[]): Promise<readonly { eventId: string; outcome: ImportOutcome; }[]>;
```

## [packages/core/src/application/server-sync.ts](../../packages/core/src/application/server-sync.ts)

Network port for the optional sync server. Implementations only move bytes; every proof, trust and
authority decision stays in the shared operations below. A failure never affects local trading.

```ts
export interface SyncTransport {
    identity(): Promise<ServerIdentity>;
    challenge(input: ChallengeRequest): Promise<Challenge>;
    enroll(input: EnrollmentRequest): Promise<void>;
    session(input: SessionRequest): Promise<Session>;
    endSession(token: string): Promise<void>;
    bootstrap(token: string): Promise<BootstrapResponse>;
    push(token: string, batch: SignedSyncBatch): Promise<PushResponse>;
    pull(token: string, cursor: string | null, limit: number): Promise<PullResponse>;
    receipt(token: string, receipt: SignedPeerReceipt): Promise<number>;
    status(token: string): Promise<SyncStatus>;
    entitlements(token: string): Promise<Entitlement>;
    plan?(token: string): Promise<Plan>;
    joinCreate(token: string, offer: JoinOffer): Promise<{
        expires_at: string;
    }>;
    joinRead(joinId: string): Promise<JoinOffer>;
    joinRequest(joinId: string, body: JoinRequestBody): Promise<void>;
    joinPollRequest(token: string, joinId: string): Promise<JoinRequestBody | null>;
    joinEnroll(token: string, joinId: string, body: JoinEnrollment): Promise<void>;
    joinPollEnrollment(joinId: string): Promise<JoinEnrollment | null>;
    joinCancel(token: string, joinId: string): Promise<void>;
}
```

Stable network failure; `retry` distinguishes backoff from a condition the owner must resolve.

```ts
class SyncTransportError extends Error {
  code: string;
  status: number;
  retry: boolean;
  constructor(code: string, status: number, retry: boolean);
}
```

Connected server, read from local SQLite; counts are this device's own delivery progress.

```ts
export interface ServerConnection {
    readonly destinationId: string;
    readonly serverDeviceId: string;
    readonly url: string;
    readonly fingerprint: string;
    readonly connectedAt: string;
    readonly lastAttemptAt: string | null;
    readonly lastSuccessAt: string | null;
    readonly lastError: string | null;
    readonly pending: number;
    readonly unsigned: number;
    readonly source: "code" | "shop";
    readonly service: Entitlement["status"] | null;
    readonly paidUntil: string | null;
    readonly graceUntil: string | null;
    readonly maxTills: number | null;
    readonly tills: number | null;
}
```

One synchronization pass. `untrusted` facts were not stored and are offered again later. `locked` counts facts
waiting for a shop key: this device's own facts it cannot seal yet and received facts it cannot open yet.

```ts
export interface SyncSummary {
    readonly pushed: number;
    readonly rejected: number;
    readonly pulled: number;
    readonly untrusted: number;
    readonly locked: number;
}
```

Normalizes a server address. HTTPS is required except on loopback and private LAN addresses,
where a self-hosted server may run without a certificate; payloads are signed either way.

```ts
normalizeServerUrl(input: string): string;
```

The short fingerprint the owner compares with the one printed by the server before trusting it.

```ts
keyFingerprint(publicKey: string, crypto: ExchangeCrypto): Promise<string>;
```

Reads and checks the server's public identity without changing anything locally.

```ts
previewServer(transport: SyncTransport, crypto: ExchangeCrypto): Promise<{ api_version: 2; server_device_id: string; public_key: string; fingerprint: string; enrollment_open: boolean; }>;
```

Connects this device to a sync server whose fingerprint came from a connection code or the owner's shared
address, so nobody retypes it. On the owner's authority-capable device this approves the server key (an
owner-signed `DEVICE_TRUSTED` fact), attaches the existing shop with its IDs using the code's single-use token,
publishes earlier pairings and shares the address with the shop's other tills. Any other device needs that owner
approval first and receives it from the server, owner-signed; it needs no token.

```ts
connectServer(context: OperationContext, transport: SyncTransport, input: {
    readonly url: string;
    readonly fingerprint: string;
    readonly token?: string;
    readonly source?: "code" | "shop";
}, crypto: ExchangeCrypto): Promise<string>;
```

Owner-signed `SERVER_ENDPOINT_SET`, so the shop's other trusted tills connect to the same server on their own.
Only the owner's authority device shares; repeating the same address records nothing.

```ts
shareServerEndpoint(context: OperationContext, input: {
    readonly serverDeviceId: string;
    readonly url: string;
    readonly fingerprint: string;
}): void;
```

The owner's shared server address when this device should connect to it on its own: set, not declined here and not already connected.

```ts
pendingShopServer(context: OperationContext): { readonly url: string; readonly fingerprint: string; readonly serverDeviceId: string; };
```

One bounded pass: announce this device's encryption key, push this device's pending facts (sealed), pull everything
others sent, then let the owner's devices create or grant shop keys. Each page is durable before its receipt is sent,
so a lost response only causes a harmless resend. A second round runs when keys changed during the first, so a
newly granted till does not wait a whole interval. Outcome and error code are recorded locally; errors are rethrown.

```ts
synchronizeServer(context: OperationContext, destinationId: string, transport: SyncTransport, crypto: SealingCrypto, options: {
    readonly batchSize?: number;
    readonly maxPages?: number;
} = {}): Promise<SyncSummary>;
```

Connected servers with local delivery counts, for status display.

```ts
serverConnections(context: OperationContext, crypto: ExchangeCrypto): Promise<readonly ServerConnection[]>;
```

Stops syncing with a server on this device and remembers the choice, so the shop's shared address does not
reconnect it. On the owner's authority device it also withdraws the shared address (`SERVER_ENDPOINT_CLEARED`);
tills already connected stay connected. Trust in the server key is unchanged; the owner revokes keys separately.

```ts
disconnectServer(context: OperationContext, destinationId: string): void;
```

True when this shop already trusts the server's key, meaning the shop was attached before (by this till or the
owner's): reconnecting then needs no single-use token.

```ts
knowsServer(context: OperationContext, serverDeviceId: string, publicKey: string): boolean;
```

True when this unlocked device may enroll the shop itself rather than waiting for the owner.

```ts
canEnrollServer(context: OperationContext): boolean;
```

## [packages/core/src/application/settings.ts](../../packages/core/src/application/settings.ts)

The shop's tax settings, readable by any unlocked member: tills need the label and rate to show receipts.

```ts
shopTaxSettings(context: OperationContext): TaxSettings;
```

A product's tax rule and the rate a sale would carry now, under products.view.

```ts
productTax(context: OperationContext, productId: string): ProductTaxRule & { readonly effectiveRateBasisPoints: number; };
```

Shop default terms for customers and suppliers; readable with customers.view, suppliers.view or business.settings.

```ts
shopPaymentTerms(context: OperationContext): { readonly customer: { readonly days: number; readonly stored: boolean; readonly version?: string; }; readonly supplier: { readonly days: number; readonly stored: boolean; readonly version?: string; }; };
```

One customer's or supplier's terms and aged balance at `now`, under customers.view or suppliers.view.

```ts
creditStatus(context: OperationContext, party: TermsParty, partyId: string, now: string): EffectiveTerms & DebtAging;
```

Customers (or suppliers) with a positive balance, aged under their terms: most overdue first, then most due soon.
Bounded to `limit` (1–500, default 100) with a `truncated` flag.

```ts
agedBalances(context: OperationContext, party: TermsParty, now: string, limit = 100): { readonly items: readonly (DebtAging & { readonly partyId: string; readonly name: string; readonly termsDays: number; })[]; readonly truncated: boolean; };
```

## [packages/core/src/application/sync-scheduler.ts](../../packages/core/src/application/sync-scheduler.ts)

Background sync for any platform, driven by the app's own timer or lifecycle events. Each `tick` first follows the
owner's shared server address if this device is not connected to it yet, then syncs every connected server. Success
waits a minute; failure backs off up to fifteen minutes. `soon` brings the next pass forward after a local change or
when the app returns to the foreground. Passes never overlap and never run without a signed-in context.

```ts
class ServerSyncScheduler {
  constructor(crypto: SealingCrypto, transport: (url: string) => SyncTransport, now: () => number = Date.now);
  /** Something changed locally, or the app came back: sync soon rather than at the next regular pass. */
  soon(): void;
  /** Runs a pass if one is due. Failures are recorded on each server's status and never thrown. */
  tick(context: OperationContext | undefined): Promise<void>;
}
```

## [packages/core/src/backup/encryption.ts](../../packages/core/src/backup/encryption.ts)

The bounds a container reader or writer checks: decompressed content and the file itself.

```ts
export type ContainerLimits = Pick<TransferLimits, "contentBytes" | "fileBytes">;
```

Who can open a new container: a password, recovery keys, or both (at least one).

```ts
export interface SealKeys {
    readonly password?: string;
    readonly recipients?: readonly RecoveryRecipient[];
}
```

What opens an existing container: its password, or a recovery key pair whose `keyId` has a slot.

```ts
export type OpenKey = {
    readonly password: string;
} | {
    readonly recoveryKey: RecoveryKeyPair;
};
```

Public facts about a container, read without any secret, for choosing how to open it.

```ts
export interface ContainerSummary {
    readonly purpose: TransferPurpose;
    readonly fileId: string;
    readonly password: boolean;
    readonly recipients: readonly string[];
    readonly sealedFor: readonly string[];
}
```

Backup/exchange passwords are independent of unlock PINs: after Unicode NFC normalization, 12–256
characters, at least 12 after trimming, not entirely digits. Returns the normalized password, so the
same password typed on a phone and a computer derives the same key (ADR-035).

```ts
normalizeTransferPassword(password: string): string;
```

Checks a password without keeping it; see .

```ts
validateTransferPassword(password: string): void;
```

Argon2id values new password slots use (provisional until the Android measurement, ADR-035).

```ts
const PASSWORD_KDF: Readonly<{ memoryKiB: 65536; passes: 3; parallelism: 1; }>;
```

Readers accept only these Argon2id ranges, so a file cannot demand unbounded work.

```ts
validPasswordKdf(params: {
    readonly memoryKiB: number;
    readonly passes: number;
    readonly parallelism: number;
}): boolean;
```

The 32-byte key-encryption key of a password slot: Argon2id over the NFC-normalized UTF-8 password.

```ts
passwordKey(password: string, salt: Uint8Array, params: {
    readonly memoryKiB: number;
    readonly passes: number;
    readonly parallelism: number;
}, crypto: TransferCrypto): Promise<Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>>;
```

True when `bytes` begin with the container magic (ADR-035).

```ts
isTransferContainer(bytes: Uint8Array): boolean;
```

Reads a container's public structure without any secret: purpose, file ID, whether it has a password
slot, the recovery keys it was sealed for when written (`sealedFor`) and those that still have a slot
(`recipients`). A `kolo.encrypted` header of another version is `unsupported_version`.

```ts
inspectContainer(file: Uint8Array): ContainerSummary;
```

The immutable content header of a container and the prefix bytes every chunk authenticates (public).

```ts
readContainerHeader(file: Uint8Array): { header: { format: "kolo.encrypted"; format_version: 3; purpose: "backup" | "enrollment" | "receipt" | "exchange"; file_id: string; cipher: "AES-256-GCM"; compression: "deflate"; nonce_prefix: string; chunk_bytes: number; content_bytes: number; recipients: string[]; }; prefix: Uint8Array<...>; };
```

Random-access bytes of a file whose size is known: a selected file on disk, or an array in memory.

```ts
export interface ByteReader {
    readonly size: number;
    read(offset: number, length: number): Promise<Uint8Array>;
}
```

A reader over bytes already in memory.

```ts
memoryReader(bytes: Uint8Array): ByteReader;
```

Writes a container (ADR-035) as a stream: the prefix and key slots first, then each 1 MiB record as soon as its
compressed bytes exist. `content` must yield exactly `contentBytes` bytes. Memory stays a few chunks whatever the size.

```ts
sealStream(content: AsyncIterable<Uint8Array>, contentBytes: number, purpose: TransferPurpose, fileId: string, keys: string | SealKeys, crypto: TransferCrypto, limits: ContainerLimits = TRANSFER_LIMITS, chunkBytes: number = CONTAINER_CHUNK_BYTES): AsyncGenerator<Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>, any, any>;
```

Deflates, then encrypts into a container (ADR-035) under a fresh file key wrapped for a password and/or recovery keys.

```ts
encryptTransfer(bytes: Uint8Array, purpose: TransferPurpose, fileId: string, keys: string | SealKeys, crypto: TransferCrypto, limits: ContainerLimits = TRANSFER_LIMITS): Promise<Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>>;
```

Opens a container as a stream of content chunks. Size, both sections, purpose, declared content length, slot parameters
and record shape are checked before any key derivation. **Chunks are provisional:** the final record, the exact content
length and the end of the deflate stream are only known at the end, and any failure then throws. A caller may only
write yielded chunks somewhere private (a staging file) and must discard them if the stream throws.

```ts
openStream(reader: ByteReader, purpose: TransferPurpose, key: string | OpenKey, crypto: TransferCrypto, limits: ContainerLimits = TRANSFER_LIMITS): AsyncGenerator<Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>, any, any>;
```

Decrypts a whole container in memory. Size, both sections, purpose, declared content length, slot parameters and
record shape are checked before any key derivation; one slot is unwrapped, then every chunk authenticates before any
content is returned, and content beyond its authenticated length is refused. Anything that is not a container,
including the JSON files of releases before ADR-035, is `invalid_file`. A password string is shorthand for `{ password }`.

```ts
decryptTransfer(file: Uint8Array, purpose: TransferPurpose, key: string | OpenKey, crypto: TransferCrypto, limits: ContainerLimits = TRANSFER_LIMITS): Promise<Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>>;
```

## [packages/core/src/backup/file-kind.ts](../../packages/core/src/backup/file-kind.ts)

What a chosen transfer file is, recognized from its structure before any password or key work.

```ts
export type TransferFileKind = "backup" | "enrollment" | "exchange" | "receipt" | "invite" | "request";
```

Recognizes a KOLO transfer file by its public structure: an encrypted container (ADR-035) or a
plaintext invitation/request. Anything else, including the JSON files of releases before ADR-035,
is `undefined`. A container of an unknown version is still recognized by purpose, so opening it
reports `unsupported_version`.

```ts
transferFileKind(bytes: Uint8Array, crypto: Pick<TransferCrypto, "decodeText">): TransferFileKind;
```

Carries an encrypted container inside a JSON transport (join mailboxes, ADR-035) as padded base64.

```ts
transferFileText(bytes: Uint8Array, crypto: Pick<TransferCrypto, "encodeBase64">): string;
```

Reverses : the text must be strict base64 of a container.

```ts
transferFileFromText(text: string, crypto: Pick<TransferCrypto, "decodeBase64">): Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
```

## [packages/core/src/backup/golden-container.ts](../../packages/core/src/backup/golden-container.ts)

The golden ADR-035 container (fixtures/golden-v3.kolo-backup) as base64, so every runtime, including the mobile
bundle, can open it in the platform self-check. A test keeps it identical to the fixture file.

```ts
const GOLDEN_CONTAINER_BASE64: string;
```

Its password; its recovery key is derived from 32 bytes of 0x42. Test material, not a secret.

```ts
const GOLDEN_PASSWORD: "golden vector password";
```

## [packages/core/src/backup/hpke.ts](../../packages/core/src/backup/hpke.ts)

RFC 9180 HPKE, base mode, single-shot, for DHKEM(X25519, HKDF-SHA256) with HKDF-SHA256 and
AES-256-GCM (KEM 0x0020, KDF 0x0001, AEAD 0x0002), built only from native primitives (ADR-035).
Key derivation is generic over the AEAD key length so the RFC's AES-128-GCM vectors also check it.

```ts
export interface HpkePrimitives {
    hmacSha256(key: Uint8Array, data: Uint8Array): Uint8Array;
    x25519(privateKey: Uint8Array, publicKey: Uint8Array): Uint8Array;
    randomHex(bytes: number): string;
    sealBytes(keyHex: string, nonceHex: string, bytes: Uint8Array, aad: Uint8Array): Promise<Uint8Array>;
    openBytes(keyHex: string, nonceHex: string, sealed: Uint8Array, aad: Uint8Array): Promise<Uint8Array>;
}
```

DER prefixes that wrap a raw 32-byte X25519 key for native key import (PKCS #8 private, SPKI public).

```ts
const X25519_PKCS8_PREFIX: readonly number[];
```

DER prefix of an X25519 SubjectPublicKeyInfo.

```ts
const X25519_SPKI_PREFIX: readonly number[];
```

Raw X25519 public key of a raw private key.

```ts
x25519PublicKey(privateKey: Uint8Array, p: Pick<HpkePrimitives, "x25519">): Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
```

RFC 9180 §7.1.3 DeriveKeyPair for X25519: a deterministic key pair from at least 32 bytes of input keying material.

```ts
deriveKeyPair(ikm: Uint8Array, p: HpkePrimitives): { readonly privateKey: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>; readonly publicKey: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>; };
```

Single-shot `SealBase` (sequence number 0): encrypts `plaintext` to a raw X25519 public key.

```ts
sealBase(recipient: Uint8Array, info: Uint8Array, aad: Uint8Array, plaintext: Uint8Array, p: HpkePrimitives, ephemeralIkm?: Uint8Array): Promise<{ readonly enc: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>; readonly ciphertext: Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>; }>;
```

Single-shot `OpenBase`; any failure is `authentication_failed`.

```ts
openBase(enc: Uint8Array, privateKey: Uint8Array, info: Uint8Array, aad: Uint8Array, ciphertext: Uint8Array, p: HpkePrimitives): Promise<Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>>;
```

## [packages/core/src/backup/native-crypto.ts](../../packages/core/src/backup/native-crypto.ts)

Narrow native AES implementation; adapters must use platform/OpenSSL APIs, never JS cryptographic substitutes.

```ts
export interface NativeTransferBackend extends Omit<TransferCrypto, "encrypt" | "decrypt" | "sealBytes" | "openBytes"> {
    cipher(keyHex: string, nonceHex: string): {
        setAAD(bytes: Uint8Array): unknown;
        update(bytes: Uint8Array): Uint8Array;
        final(): Uint8Array;
        getAuthTag(): Uint8Array;
    };
    decipher(keyHex: string, nonceHex: string): {
        setAAD(bytes: Uint8Array): unknown;
        setAuthTag(bytes: Uint8Array): unknown;
        update(bytes: Uint8Array): Uint8Array;
        final(): Uint8Array;
    };
    fromHex(hex: string): Uint8Array;
    toHex(bytes: Uint8Array): string;
}
```

Consistent native AES-GCM framing and byte validation across desktop/mobile, with no dependency on either SDK.

```ts
nativeTransferCrypto(native: NativeTransferBackend): TransferCrypto;
```

## [packages/core/src/backup/recovery-key.ts](../../packages/core/src/backup/recovery-key.ts)

The shop backup recovery key (ADR-036): 32 random bytes, written once for the owner as 56 Crockford
base32 symbols (52 for the secret, 4 checksum) in 14 groups of four. Its HPKE key pair opens the
recipient slots of backups; tills keep only the public half.

```ts
export interface RecoveryKeyPair {
    readonly keyId: string;
    readonly publicKey: Uint8Array;
    readonly privateKey: Uint8Array;
}
```

A recipient a backup is sealed to: the pinned public half of a recovery key.

```ts
export interface RecoveryRecipient {
    readonly keyId: string;
    readonly publicKey: Uint8Array;
}
```

A fresh recovery secret from the native random generator.

```ts
generateRecoverySecret(crypto: Pick<TransferCrypto, "randomHex">): Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
```

The recovery key's stable ID: the first 16 bytes, in hex, of SHA-256("kolo.backup-recovery-key.v1\0" ‖ public key).

```ts
recoveryKeyId(publicKey: Uint8Array, crypto: Pick<TransferCrypto, "sha256Bytes">): Promise<string>;
```

The recovery key pair of a secret: RFC 9180 DeriveKeyPair, so S is used for nothing else.

```ts
deriveRecoveryKey(secret: Uint8Array, crypto: TransferCrypto): Promise<RecoveryKeyPair>;
```

Writes a secret for the owner: `XXXX-XXXX-…`, 14 groups of four Crockford base32 symbols.

```ts
formatRecoveryKey(secret: Uint8Array, crypto: Pick<TransferCrypto, "sha256Bytes">): Promise<string>;
```

Reads a typed recovery key: case, spaces and hyphens are ignored and O, I and L read as 0, 1 and 1.
A wrong length, a non-zero padding bit or a checksum mismatch is `invalid_recovery_key`, before any
decryption is attempted.

```ts
parseRecoveryKey(text: string, crypto: Pick<TransferCrypto, "sha256Bytes">): Promise<Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>>;
```

## [packages/core/src/backup/snapshot.ts](../../packages/core/src/backup/snapshot.ts)

Current journal/legacy coverage derived from this exact snapshot, not a live database's later cursor.

```ts
snapshotBoundary(tx: Queryable, businessId: string): { audit_watermark: number; applied_count: number; origins: { device_id: string; maximum_sequence: number; next_local_sequence?: number; }[]; legacy: { business_id: string; provenance: "unsigned_v1_snapshot"; outbox_next: number; audit_watermark: number; }[]; };
```

Checks the exact shipped SQLite schema before executing any imported schema-triggered mutation, then, once migrated,
the business consistency invariants; records that disagree fail with `inconsistent_records`.

```ts
validateSnapshot(database: Database, manifest: SnapshotManifest, files: SnapshotPort): Promise<void>;
```

Packs a small signed manifest plus raw SQLite bytes, avoiding double-base64 expansion.

```ts
encodeSnapshot(signed: SignedSnapshot, bytes: Uint8Array, crypto: TransferCrypto): Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>;
```

Bounds metadata/image independently before schema parsing or opening SQLite.

```ts
decodeSnapshot(bytes: Uint8Array, crypto: TransferCrypto): { signed: { manifest: { format: "kolo.snapshot"; format_version: 1; snapshot_id: string; kind: "backup" | "enrollment"; schema_version: number; created_at: string; business: { id: string; name: string; currency: "XAF"; }; ... 8 more ...; shop_keys?: { ...; }[]; }; digest: string; signature: string; }; image: Uint8Ar...;
```

## [packages/core/src/backup/types.ts](../../packages/core/src/backup/types.ts)

Bounded portable snapshot; native readers enforce this before allocating file contents.

```ts
const MAX_SNAPSHOT_BYTES: number;
```

Maximum encrypted file on disk. Content is deflated before encryption.

```ts
const MAX_TRANSFER_FILE_BYTES: number;
```

Same measured candidate policy as local credentials; Android/Windows acceptance remains a native gate.

```ts
const TRANSFER_KDF_ITERATIONS: 600000;
```

Stable recovery/transfer failures without customer data, passwords or native exception text.

```ts
class TransferError extends Error {
  code: "invalid_file" | "too_large" | "invalid_password" | "authentication_failed" | "unsupported_version" | "invalid_snapshot" | "invalid_recovery_key" | "recovery_key_required" | "untrusted_backup" | "scope_mismatch" | "confirmation_required" | "not_empty" | "pairing_pending" | "pairing_expired" | "pairing_conflict" | "invalid_pairing" | "activation_failed" | "cancelled" | "sync_keys_unavailable" | "inconsistent_records";
  constructor(code: TransferError["code"]);
}
```

Argon2id cost and output parameters; `secret` and `associatedData` exist for the RFC 9106 test vector.

```ts
export interface Argon2Params {
    readonly memoryKiB: number;
    readonly passes: number;
    readonly parallelism: number;
    readonly length: number;
    readonly secret?: Uint8Array;
    readonly associatedData?: Uint8Array;
}
```

Native PBKDF2/AES-256-GCM/SHA-256 and byte codecs. Private installation keys stay in CredentialPort.

```ts
export interface TransferCrypto extends ExchangeCrypto {
    randomHex(bytes: number): string;
    derive(password: string, saltHex: string, iterations: number): Promise<string>;
    sha256Bytes(bytes: Uint8Array): Promise<string>;
    encrypt(keyHex: string, nonceHex: string, bytes: Uint8Array, aad: string): Promise<{
        ciphertext: string;
        tag: string;
    }>;
    decrypt(keyHex: string, nonceHex: string, ciphertext: string, tagHex: string, aad: string): Promise<Uint8Array>;
    sealBytes(keyHex: string, nonceHex: string, bytes: Uint8Array, aad: Uint8Array): Promise<Uint8Array>;
    openBytes(keyHex: string, nonceHex: string, sealed: Uint8Array, aad: Uint8Array): Promise<Uint8Array>;
    hmacSha256(key: Uint8Array, data: Uint8Array): Uint8Array;
    x25519(privateKey: Uint8Array, publicKey: Uint8Array): Uint8Array;
    createSha256(): {
        update(bytes: Uint8Array): void;
        digest(): string;
    };
    argon2id(password: Uint8Array, salt: Uint8Array, params: Argon2Params): Promise<Uint8Array>;
    encodeText(text: string): Uint8Array;
    decodeText(bytes: Uint8Array): string;
    encodeBase64(bytes: Uint8Array): string;
    decodeBase64(text: string): Uint8Array;
}
```

A compacted snapshot image on private storage, read in chunks; `close` deletes it.

```ts
export interface ImageReader {
    readonly size: number;
    read(offset: number, length: number): Promise<Uint8Array>;
    close(): Promise<void>;
}
```

A private staging file being written chunk by chunk; `open` checks its size and opens it, `discard` deletes it.

```ts
export interface ImageWriter {
    write(bytes: Uint8Array): Promise<void>;
    open(): Promise<SnapshotHandle>;
    discard(): Promise<void>;
}
```

Where a streamed transfer file goes: a private temporary file, or memory in tests.

```ts
export interface ByteSink {
    write(bytes: Uint8Array): Promise<void>;
}
```

Isolated private staging database; activation must durably compare-and-switch the target's current slot.

```ts
export interface SnapshotHandle {
    readonly database: Database;
    bytes(): Promise<Uint8Array>;
    image(): Promise<ImageReader>;
    discard(): Promise<void>;
    activate(expectedTarget: Database): Promise<Database>;
}
```

Platform boundary for SQLite-consistent copies and bounded private staging/activation.

```ts
export interface SnapshotPort {
    capture(source: Database): Promise<SnapshotHandle>;
    open(bytes: Uint8Array): Promise<SnapshotHandle>;
    receive(): Promise<ImageWriter>;
    empty(): Promise<SnapshotHandle>;
}
```

A file ready for a user-selected destination: an encrypted container (ADR-035) or a
plaintext UTF-8 JSON invitation/request. Hosts write `bytes` unchanged.

```ts
export interface TransferDocument {
    readonly filename: string;
    readonly bytes: Uint8Array;
}
```

## [packages/core/src/data/auth/login.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/auth/login.ts)

What someone types to log in, plus what this installation is.

```ts
export interface Credentials {
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly phone: string;
    readonly pin: string;
    readonly device: {
        readonly id?: string | undefined;
        readonly label?: string | undefined;
        readonly platform?: string | undefined;
    };
}
```

What logging in needs from the server.

```ts
export interface AuthTransport {
    login(credentials: Credentials): Promise<Enrolment>;
}
```

Why a login did not succeed.

```ts
export type LoginProblem = "invalid_credentials" | "account_suspended" | "device_suspended" | "business_suspended" | "too_many_attempts" | "invalid_request" | "unreachable" | "server_error";
```

Raised when a login fails.

It carries a code rather than a sentence because the screen speaks French and
English (§44), and a client that matched on the server's English prose would
break the moment it was translated.

```ts
class LoginError extends Error {
  code: LoginProblem;
  /** How long to wait before trying again, when the server said. */
  retryAfterSeconds: number | undefined;
  constructor(code: LoginProblem, message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
    retryAfterSeconds?: number | undefined;
});
}
```

Logs in and enrols this device, returning the session it may now trade under.

The device identity comes from what this installation already holds rather
than from the caller, so a shift change reuses the till's identity instead of
registering a new one.

```ts
logIn(database: Database, secrets: SecretStore, transport: AuthTransport, credentials: Credentials, now: Date = new Date()): Promise<Session>;
```

What an HTTP login needs to know.

```ts
export interface HttpAuthTransportOptions {
    readonly baseUrl: string;
    readonly fetch?: typeof globalThis.fetch;
    readonly timeoutMs?: number;
}
```

How long a login may take before it is abandoned.

```ts
const DEFAULT_LOGIN_TIMEOUT_MS: 30000;
```

A transport that logs in over HTTP.

```ts
class HttpAuthTransport implements AuthTransport {
  constructor(options: HttpAuthTransportOptions);
  login(credentials: Credentials): Promise<Enrolment>;
}
```

## [packages/core/src/data/auth/secure-store.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/auth/secure-store.ts)

Where the session token is kept (§37, ADR-012).

The token is the one thing on the device that is a *credential* rather than a
record. Everything else in `kolo.db` (sales, prices, what a customer owes)
describes what happened; the token lets whoever holds it act as this till
against the server. §37 asks for secure token storage, so it goes to the
platform keystore rather than into a column beside the business data.

## Why the module is passed in

This file does not import `expo-secure-store`, for the reason `expo-sqlite.ts`
does not import `expo-sqlite` (ADR-016). It describes the three methods the
data layer needs and takes them as an argument; `store-on-device.ts` hands
the real module over on one line. So "expo-secure-store's API looks like
this" is checked by the compiler at exactly one call site, and everything
above can be tested under Node without a device.

What no amount of Node testing covers is the keystore itself, which needs a
device: the same honest gap ADR-016 states for SQLite.

The slice of the native secure store this adapter uses.

```ts
export interface NativeSecureStore {
    getItemAsync(key: string): Promise<string | null>;
    setItemAsync(key: string, value: string): Promise<void>;
    deleteItemAsync(key: string): Promise<void>;
}
```

A place to keep a secret.

Asynchronous, unlike the database, because the keystore genuinely is: reading
it can involve the platform's key manager. That is why the token is *not*
part of ; a session is read synchronously while drawing a
screen, and only the sync engine needs the token.

```ts
export interface SecretStore {
    read(key: string): Promise<string | undefined>;
    write(key: string, secret: string): Promise<void>;
    forget(key: string): Promise<void>;
}
```

The key the session token is stored under.

```ts
const SESSION_TOKEN_KEY: "kolo.session.token";
```

Raised when the keystore could not be read or written.

```ts
class SecretStoreError extends Error {
  /** The key involved, which is never itself a secret. */
  key: string;
  constructor(message: string, key: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Wraps a native secure store as a .

The translations are small and both are deliberate: `null` becomes
`undefined`, so callers have one way to say "nothing stored" (the same
translation `expo-sqlite.ts` makes for a missing row), and a thrown platform
error becomes a  that names the key but never the
value: an error that reached a log carrying the token would defeat the point
of storing it here.

```ts
nativeSecretStore(native: NativeSecureStore): SecretStore;
```

A  held in memory, for tests.

It is here rather than in a test file because both the tests of this layer
and the tests of everything above it need one, and two copies would drift.
Nothing in the app builds one: a device that kept its token in memory would
ask for a PIN on every restart.

```ts
memorySecretStore(): SecretStore & { readonly size: () => number; };
```

## [packages/core/src/data/auth/session.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/auth/session.ts)

The identity every write is recorded against.

```ts
export interface Session {
    readonly sessionId: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly businessName: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly userName: string;
    readonly role: string;
    readonly permissions: readonly string[];
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly expiresAt: string;
}
```

What a successful login yields, in the shape the server sends it.

```ts
export interface Enrolment {
    readonly token: string;
    readonly business: {
        readonly id: string;
        readonly name: string;
        readonly currency: string;
    };
    readonly branches: ReadonlyArray<{
        readonly id: string;
        readonly name: string;
    }>;
    readonly user: {
        readonly id: string;
        readonly name: string;
        readonly role: string;
        readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
        readonly permissions: readonly string[];
    };
    readonly device: {
        readonly id: string;
        readonly label: string;
        readonly platform: string;
        readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    };
    readonly session: {
        readonly id: string;
        readonly issuedAt: string;
        readonly expiresAt: string;
    };
}
```

Raised when a login cannot be stored on this device.

```ts
class EnrolmentError extends Error {
  /** A stable code, because the screen speaks French and English (§44). */
  code: EnrolmentProblem;
  constructor(code: EnrolmentProblem, message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

What can stop a login being stored.

```ts
export type EnrolmentProblem = "another_business" | "unsupported_currency";
```

Stores a login, so the device is enrolled and can trade offline.

The order is deliberate: **the token is written first**, then the rows. If
the second half fails the device is left with a secret nothing reads, which
the next login overwrites; the other order would leave a device that looks
enrolled and cannot authenticate: a till that appears to work and whose
every sync is refused.

```ts
enrol(database: Database, secrets: SecretStore, enrolment: Enrolment, now: Date = new Date()): Promise<Session>;
```

Reads the session this device holds, or undefined if it is not enrolled.

Undefined is a real answer, not a failure: an app installed and never logged
in has no business and no user, and inventing one would be worse than saying
so: it would attribute a shop's sales to a user nobody registered.

An expired session is still returned. Whether the device may keep trading on
it is a decision for the screen above, and one this layer must not make
silently: a till that went blank because a token lapsed overnight would lose
the shop its morning.

```ts
storedSession(database: Queryable): Session;
```

Whether a session has lapsed at the given moment.

```ts
hasExpired(session: Session, now: Date = new Date()): boolean;
```

Whether the session holds a permission (§38).

A lookup, not a rule: the server resolved role defaults, grants and
revocations when it issued the session, and re-deriving that here would hand
back a permission the server had revoked; a role's defaults would put it
straight back.

```ts
may(session: Session, permission: string): boolean;
```

The token to present to the server, or undefined if there is none.

```ts
sessionToken(secrets: SecretStore): Promise<string>;
```

Ends the session on this device, keeping everything the shop recorded.

The business data stays deliberately. A till may have a day of sales that
have not reached the server, and signing out is not a decision to discard
them: the next login on the same business finds them still queued.

The order mirrors : the row goes first, so a keystore that fails
leaves a secret nothing reads rather than a device that still looks enrolled.

```ts
signOut(database: Database, secrets: SecretStore): Promise<void>;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/customers/customers.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/customers/customers.ts)

The event a customer write queues.

```ts
const CUSTOMER_SAVED: "CUSTOMER_SAVED";
```

The event a payment against a customer's account queues.

```ts
const CUSTOMER_PAYMENT: "CUSTOMER_PAYMENT";
```

A customer as the device holds them.

```ts
export interface Customer {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly phone?: string | undefined;
    readonly address?: string | undefined;
    readonly notes?: string | undefined;
    readonly creditLimitMinor?: number | undefined;
    readonly active: boolean;
    readonly createdAt: string;
    readonly updatedAt: string;
    readonly updatedByDevice?: string | undefined;
}
```

What a caller supplies when saving a customer.

```ts
export interface SaveCustomer extends Customer {
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
}
```

What a customer owes, worked out from their ledger.

```ts
export interface Balance {
    readonly outstandingMinor: number;
    readonly entries: number;
}
```

Raised when a customer cannot be saved or read.

```ts
class CustomerError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Saves a customer and queues the event carrying them to the server.

Both in one transaction (ADR-014). A customer the shopkeeper can see and the
server never will is worse here than for a product: a credit sale to them
would be refused everywhere else in the shop.

Returns whether the write took effect. A local save normally does, but it can
lose to an edit from another device that arrived with a later timestamp, and
the caller should show what is stored rather than what was typed.
Application adapters supply a native UUIDv7 event ID; omitted IDs retain the
legacy randomUUID behavior for existing repository callers.

```ts
saveCustomer(database: Database, customer: SaveCustomer, eventId?: string): boolean;
```

Writes a customer, keeping whichever edit wins.

Shared by a local save and by an edit arriving from another device, so both
are resolved by the same comparison.

```ts
upsertCustomer(tx: Queryable, customer: Customer, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

A customer handing over money against what they owe.

```ts
export interface CustomerPayment {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly customerId: string;
    readonly accountId: string;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
    readonly note?: string | undefined;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly recordedAt: string;
    readonly eventId: string;
}
```

Records a customer paying off some or all of what they owe (§13).

The entry and the event that carries it in one transaction (ADR-014), like
every other write here: a payment the shopkeeper has taken and the server
never hears of would leave the customer owing money they have already handed
over.

Payments are against the account, not against a sale. A regular's debt is
built from a fortnight of small buys, and asking a cashier which of them the
5,000 pays off would be inventing a question the shop does not ask.

Nothing is checked against the balance. Paying more than is owed leaves a
customer in credit, which is the honest answer; refusing it would turn away
money the shopkeeper has in their hand.

```ts
recordCustomerPayment(database: Database, payment: CustomerPayment): void;
```

The change a customer kept on account at a sale (`KeptChange` in the application operations): the customer
payments noted with the sale's number at the sale's exact time. Zero when there are none.

```ts
changeKeptOnAccount(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, sale: {
    readonly customerId: string | null;
    readonly number: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
}): number;
```

Returns a customer, or undefined if this device does not hold them.

```ts
customerOf(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, id: string): Customer;
```

Lists a business's customers, by name.

```ts
listCustomers(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, options: {
    readonly includeClosed?: boolean;
} = {}): Customer[];
```

Finds everyone registered to a number.

Several, deliberately: a number is how a customer is found at the counter but
it is not an identity. A household shares one phone, and returning a single
record would be picking one of them arbitrarily.

```ts
customersByPhone(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, phone: string): Customer[];
```

What a customer owes: the sum of their ledger, never a stored number (§13).

Summed by SQLite rather than by loading every entry, because a regular who has
bought on credit for two years has hundreds and a till asking what they owe
must not pay for all of them (§46).

```ts
balanceOf(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, customerId: string): Balance;
```

Whether a further amount would put a customer past their limit.

A question, not a rule this layer enforces. A shopkeeper who lets a regular go
over their limit is making a business decision, and §13 lists limits alongside
statements as information. What a caller must not do is ask and then ignore
the answer silently.

A customer with no limit set is never over it: no limit means the shopkeeper
has not decided, and refusing on that basis would block every regular who has
never been given one.

```ts
wouldExceedCreditLimit(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, customer: Customer, furtherMinor: number): boolean;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/backup-key-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/backup-key-migration.ts)

Shop backup recovery keys (ADR-036): the public halves the owner pinned through signed `SHOP_BACKUP_KEY` facts.
Business data, so snapshots carry it; the secret is never stored anywhere.

```ts
const BACKUP_KEY_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/changes.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/changes.ts)

Query domains invalidated after a successful SQLite commit; no business records are cached.

```ts
export type ChangedDomain = "products" | "inventory" | "sales" | "customers" | "suppliers" | "purchases" | "accounts" | "expenses" | "authority" | "settings" | "notifications";
```

Subscribes to committed changes. Unsubscribe on screen unmount/database replacement.

```ts
subscribeChanges(database: Database, domains: readonly ChangedDomain[], notify: () => void): () => void;
```

Stable snapshot for useSyncExternalStore; read again after a notification.

```ts
changeRevision(database: Database, domains: readonly ChangedDomain[]): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/credential-recovery-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/credential-recovery-migration.ts)

Durable native/SQLite credential and staff provisioning gaps; no secret values.

```ts
const CREDENTIAL_RECOVERY_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/custom-roles-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/custom-roles-migration.ts)

Shop-defined roles: a named copy of a built-in staff role with its own permissions, set through signed
`CUSTOM_ROLE_SET` facts. A deleted role stays as a tombstone so a late fact cannot bring it back and a member who
still holds it keeps the same access. A membership's `custom_role_id` points at the role it holds; its `role`
column is then that role's base, so every check of the built-in role keeps working. Business data, so snapshots
carry it.

```ts
const CUSTOM_ROLES_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/driver.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/driver.ts)

The narrow surface the data layer needs from a SQLite driver.

The application runs a native SQLite module; tests run `node:sqlite`. The
same SQL executes against both, so a query is exercised for real rather than
mocked, the same reasoning that puts the server's repository tests against a
real PostgreSQL (ADR-013, ADR-014). Only this thin adapter differs.

It is deliberately small. Anything richer would be a second query builder to
keep working, and would tempt the data layer into depending on one driver's
conveniences.

A value SQLite can store.

```ts
export type SqlValue = string | number | bigint | Uint8Array | null;
```

A row as returned by a query, keyed by column name.

```ts
export type Row = Record<string, SqlValue>;
```

What a write reports back.

```ts
export interface WriteResult {
    readonly changes: number;
}
```

A connection that can read and write.

Every method takes the full SQL and its parameters. Parameters are always
bound, never interpolated: a product name with an apostrophe in it is
ordinary in this market, and string-built SQL would break on it long before
anyone tried anything malicious.

```ts
export interface Queryable {
    run(sql: string, parameters?: readonly SqlValue[]): WriteResult;
    all(sql: string, parameters?: readonly SqlValue[]): Row[];
    get(sql: string, parameters?: readonly SqlValue[]): Row | undefined;
}
```

A database.

`transaction` is the only way to write. ADR-014 requires a business write and
its outbox event to commit together, and a connection that could be written
to directly would make it possible to write one without the other.

```ts
export interface Database extends Queryable {
    transaction<T>(work: (tx: Queryable) => T): T;
    close(): void;
}
```

Raised when the driver rejects a statement.

It carries the SQL, because a constraint violation reported without the
statement that caused it is close to undiagnosable. Parameters are
deliberately not carried: they hold business data, and an error that reaches
a log should not take a customer's phone number with it.

```ts
class DatabaseError extends Error {
  sql: string;
  constructor(message: string, sql: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/event-journal-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/event-journal-migration.ts)

Append-only v2 facts/signatures plus mutable, destination-scoped delivery/application state.

```ts
const EVENT_JOURNAL_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/expo-sqlite.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/expo-sqlite.ts)

The slice of a native SQLite handle this adapter uses.

Synchronous throughout, which ADR-016 makes a requirement rather than a
preference: `Database.transaction` runs a function to completion so that a
business write and its outbox event commit together (ADR-014), and a promise
in the middle of that is a seam a caller could break.

```ts
export interface NativeDatabase {
    execSync(source: string): void;
    runSync(source: string, params: NativeBindValue[]): {
        readonly changes: number;
    };
    getAllSync(source: string, params: NativeBindValue[]): unknown[];
    getFirstSync(source: string, params: NativeBindValue[]): unknown;
    closeSync(): void;
}
```

What the native driver can bind, which is **not** quite .

The difference is `bigint`, and it matters. The data layer's value type allows
one because `node:sqlite` hands back a bigint for an integer too large for a
double; the native driver has no such case and refuses it. Rather than widen
the interface to a lie or narrow `SqlValue` and lose what tests read back,
 converts at this boundary and refuses what cannot survive it.

The list is mutable because the driver's own signature is, and a readonly
array is not assignable to it.

```ts
export type NativeBindValue = string | number | boolean | null | Uint8Array | ArrayBuffer;
```

Wraps a native handle, applying the same settings the test adapter uses.

The pragmas are repeated rather than shared because they are per-connection
and this is a different connection, and because a device that silently ran
without foreign keys would behave differently from every test, which is the
one divergence that would be hardest to find.

```ts
openNativeDatabase(connection: NativeDatabase): Database;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/full-text-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/full-text-migration.ts)

Word search (migration 24).

An FTS5 index over product, customer and supplier text, folded for case and accents
(`unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`), so a word anywhere in the name, brand, description, phone or
contact matches, not only the start of the name. Triggers keep it in step with every write,
including replayed events and restored backups; the prefix search on `search_name` remains.

```ts
const FULL_TEXT_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

Rebuilds the word-search index from the tables it covers. Backups leave the index out (it is
derived and would only enlarge the file), so restore and pairing rebuild it here.

```ts
rebuildFullText(tx: Queryable): void;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/hosted-service-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/hosted-service-migration.ts)

Forward-only: what a connected server last said about this shop's hosted service (ADR-031), so the till can warn
before sync pauses and explain why it did. Local trading never reads it.

```ts
const HOSTED_SERVICE_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/integrity.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/integrity.ts)

Read-only consistency checks over one local database: the invariants every write path must keep, plus sync states
that need a person's attention. Used by `kolo check` and asserted after replay, sync and property tests.

`corruption` findings must never occur: SQLite damage, broken references, fan-out that disagrees with its source
(stock movements vs sale/purchase lines, credit-sale debt vs the sale's own arithmetic, returns beyond the original),
cross-shop references and journal bookkeeping. `attention` findings are legitimate operational states (quarantined
or unsupported facts, work stuck for longer than `stuckHours`) that tests may create on purpose. Negative stock is
valid offline history and never reported.

```ts
export interface IntegrityFinding {
    readonly code: string;
    readonly count: number;
    readonly severity: "corruption" | "attention";
}
```

```ts
export interface IntegrityReport {
    readonly ok: boolean;
    readonly consistent: boolean;
    readonly schemaVersion: number;
    readonly issues: readonly IntegrityFinding[];
    readonly sync: {
        readonly application: readonly Row[];
        readonly delivery: readonly Row[];
        readonly outbox: readonly Row[];
        readonly stuckHours: number;
    } | Record<string, never>;
}
```

Runs every check; refuses nothing and changes nothing. Databases at another schema version report only that.

```ts
checkIntegrity(db: Queryable, options: {
    readonly now?: Date;
    readonly stuckHours?: number;
} = {}): IntegrityReport;
```

Throws with the findings when a database holds any corruption; attention findings are allowed. For tests and tools.

```ts
assertConsistent(db: Queryable, label = "database"): void;
```

Test teardown: asserts every database is consistent, then closes them all (also when one fails). A database left at
another schema version on purpose (upgrade tests) is only closed. Reports every inconsistent database at once.

```ts
closeConsistent(databases: readonly {
    close(): void;
    get: Queryable["get"];
    all: Queryable["all"];
    run: Queryable["run"];
}[]): void;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/local-authority-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/local-authority-migration.ts)

Forward-only F1 schema. Pending setup stores intent, never credentials.

```ts
const LOCAL_AUTHORITY_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/local-network-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/local-network-migration.ts)

Per-installation local network settings and direct-peer delivery status; never copied into backups/pairing snapshots.

```ts
const LOCAL_NETWORK_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/migrations.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/migrations.ts)

One step of the schema's history.

```ts
export interface Migration {
    readonly version: number;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly statements: readonly string[];
}
```

The schema's history, in order.

A migration here is permanent once released: a device in the field has
already applied it, and editing it would mean two devices disagree about what
version 1 was. Changes are made by appending.

```ts
const MIGRATIONS: readonly Migration[];
```

The version the code in this build expects.

```ts
const LATEST_VERSION: number;
```

Raised when the schema cannot be brought up to date.

```ts
class MigrationError extends Error {
  version: number;
  constructor(message: string, version: number, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

What a migration run did.

```ts
export interface MigrationResult {
    readonly from: number;
    readonly to: number;
    readonly applied: readonly Migration[];
}
```

Brings the schema up to the latest version, applying only what is missing.

Each migration runs in its own transaction, so a failure part-way leaves the
schema at the last version that fully applied rather than somewhere between
two.

```ts
migrate(database: Database): MigrationResult;
```

Returns the version the database is currently at; zero if none applied.

```ts
schemaVersion(database: Queryable): number;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/node-sqlite.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/node-sqlite.ts)

Options for opening a database.

```ts
export interface OpenOptions {
    readonly readOnly?: boolean;
    readonly location?: string;
}
```

Opens a SQLite database with the settings this application needs.

```ts
openDatabase(options: OpenOptions = {}): Database;
```

Native file slots and private SQLite staging, shared by desktop and real-database recovery tests.

```ts
nodeWorkspaceFiles(directory: string): SnapshotPort & { openActive(): Database; activeId(): string; saved(): Promise<readonly { id: string; name: string; lastUsedAt: string; }[]>; selectSaved(id: string, target: Database): Promise<...>; close(): void; };
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/photos-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/photos-migration.ts)

Optional product photos (migration 22).

One current thumbnail per product, held as a small BLOB beside the catalogue so a
backup snapshot carries it without a second store. The digest is the SHA-256 of the
bytes, the content address later transfers and server storage will use. Size is
bounded per row here and per shop by the operation that writes it.

```ts
const PHOTOS_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/product-categories-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/product-categories-migration.ts)

Additional product categories (migration 23).

A product keeps one main category on its own row (`products.category_id`), which sales and
reports group by so category totals still add up to sales. Further categories are a separate
set, used for browsing and filtering only, with its own last-write-wins version so an older
till that does not know the set keeps applying ordinary product edits.

```ts
const PRODUCT_CATEGORIES_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/returns-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/returns-migration.ts)

Forward-only append-only partial returns, linked to original immutable sales/purchases and predecessor.

```ts
const RETURNS_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/review-policy-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/review-policy-migration.ts)

Forward-only review and acceptance policy (ADR-040–042):
- `accepted_through`/`accepted_revision`: how far the owner accepted a revoked device's history
  (`DEVICE_HISTORY_ACCEPTED`); later facts wait for review instead of being refused.
- `event_exception_reviews`: the owner's approval of one exact fact for a reason the older `event_reviews` (authority
  only) does not cover: a revoked device beyond its cutoff, or a return that raced another.
- `return_fork_links`: the predecessor an approved concurrent return was signed against, when the local chain had
  to link it after a different return.
- `membership_revisions`: each member's role, overrides and state as they stood at every earlier revision, captured
  by trigger whenever a revision changes, so a fact signed under an older revision is judged by what it allowed.
- Line product guards on sale and delivery lines.

```ts
const REVIEW_POLICY_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/role-permissions-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/role-permissions-migration.ts)

The shop's role matrix (ADR-037): each staff role's permissions as the owner set them through signed
`ROLE_PERMISSIONS_SET` facts. No row, or a NULL list, means the role's built-in defaults, so existing shops keep
exactly the access they had. Business data, so snapshots carry it.

```ts
const ROLE_PERMISSIONS_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/safe-values.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/safe-values.ts)

Checks SQLite numeric output before exposing money, quantity or counts to JavaScript callers.

```ts
sqlInteger(value: unknown): number;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/sealed-sync-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/sealed-sync-migration.ts)

Forward-only end-to-end encrypted sync (ADR-030): each device's X25519 key, owner grants of shop keys sealed
to those keys, the shop's chosen server address, and how this device came to be connected. Shop keys are
never stored in the clear: a device re-opens its own grants with the private key held by its native vault.

```ts
const SEALED_SYNC_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/search-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/search-migration.ts)

Forward-only search keys. `search_name` is `name` folded once at write time by the same rules as
a typed term (see `search.ts`), so searches compare plain columns instead of folding every row on
every keystroke. Triggers keep it current for every writer, including replayed events and restores.
The generated SQL is pinned by a digest in the migration tests: changing the fold rules needs a new
migration, never an edit here.

```ts
const SEARCH_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/search.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/search.ts)

SQL expression for `column` with the same folding as . `column` must be a trusted identifier.

```ts
foldedColumn(column: string): string;
```

Lower-cases and removes accents from a typed search term.

```ts
foldSearchTerm(term: string): string;
```

A folded term escaped for `LIKE ... ESCAPE '\'`: a prefix by default, or a match anywhere.

```ts
likePattern(term: string, { anywhere = false }: {
    readonly anywhere?: boolean;
} = {}): string;
```

An FTS5 query for a typed term: every word must match, the last one as a prefix while it is
still being typed ("sac riz parf" finds "Sac de riz parfumé"). Only letters and digits reach the query, so operators, quotes and
column filters typed by a user are inert. Returns undefined when nothing searchable remains.

```ts
fullTextQuery(term: string): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/server-sync-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/server-sync-migration.ts)

Forward-only optional-server support: a `SERVER` device platform, the owner-signed roster revision of each
trusted key, and the endpoint of a connected sync server. The devices table is rebuilt to widen its platform
check without rewriting child references.

```ts
const SERVER_SYNC_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/tax-terms-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/tax-terms-migration.ts)

Forward-only tax, payment terms and notification state (ADR-033).

Tax settings, product tax rules and payment terms are last-write-wins records carried by their own signed events, so
an older till keeps those events unapplied instead of rejecting a changed payload. Immutable ledgers are untouched:
due dates and tax amounts are derived. `notification_state` is this device's read/announced marks, never synced.

```ts
const TAX_TERMS_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/till-plan-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/till-plan-migration.ts)

Forward-only: the till limit a connected server last reported for this shop (ADR-039), beside its hosted service
status, so the till can say "your plan allows N tills" and explain a waiting till. `plan_tills` null means the server
has not said (or predates the plan endpoint); `plan_max_tills` null with a count means unlimited. Display only.

```ts
const TILL_PLAN_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/database/transfer-migration.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/database/transfer-migration.ts)

Forward-only pairing progress: public proofs/opaque native references, never passwords or private keys.

```ts
const TRANSFER_MIGRATION: Migration;
```

Appends a validated immutable envelope; transaction ownership belongs to the caller.
