## [packages/core/src/data/expenses/expenses.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/expenses/expenses.ts)

The event an expense queues.

```ts
const EXPENSE_RECORDED: "EXPENSE_RECORDED";
```

What a cost was for (§19).

```ts
export type Category = "RENT" | "ELECTRICITY" | "WATER" | "TRANSPORT" | "SALARY" | "PACKAGING" | "MAINTENANCE" | "INTERNET" | "MARKETING" | "OTHER";
```

Money the business spent.

```ts
export interface Expense {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly category: Category;
    readonly accountId: string;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
    readonly description?: string | undefined;
    readonly reference?: string | undefined;
    readonly reversesId?: string | undefined;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly recordedAt: string;
    readonly eventId: string;
}
```

What a period cost, broken down the way §19 asks for.

```ts
export interface CategoryTotal {
    readonly category: Category;
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly count: number;
}
```

Raised when an expense cannot be recorded or read.

```ts
class ExpenseError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Records an expense and queues the event carrying it.

Both in one transaction (ADR-014): a cost the shopkeeper can see and the
server never will would make the month's takings look better than they were.

```ts
recordExpense(database: Database, expense: Expense): void;
```

Builds the expense that undoes another.

It keeps the original's category and account, because it has to land back
where the money came from to be worth anything, and inherits what the original
said so a report reads as a pair.

```ts
reverseExpense(original: Expense, correction: Pick<Expense, "id" | "userId" | "deviceId" | "recordedAt" | "eventId"> & Partial<Pick<Expense, "description" | "occurredAt">>): Expense;
```

The expenses of a period, by when the money was spent.

```ts
expensesBetween(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): Expense[];
```

What a period cost, by category, largest first.

Reversals are negative and cancel what they undo by plain addition, so a cost
entered twice and corrected does not show as spending that happened.

```ts
totalsByCategory(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): CategoryTotal[];
```

## [packages/core/src/data/finance/accounts.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/finance/accounts.ts)

The event an account write queues.

```ts
const ACCOUNT_SAVED: "ACCOUNT_SAVED";
```

What sort of place money sits in (§10, §17).

```ts
export type AccountKind = "CASH" | "MOBILE_MONEY" | "BANK" | "CARD" | "OTHER";
```

An account as the device holds it.

```ts
export interface Account {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly kind: AccountKind;
    readonly currency: string;
    readonly number?: string | undefined;
    readonly notes?: string | undefined;
    readonly active: boolean;
    readonly createdAt: string;
    readonly updatedAt: string;
    readonly updatedByDevice?: string | undefined;
}
```

What a caller supplies when saving an account.

```ts
export interface SaveAccount extends Account {
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
}
```

Raised when an account cannot be saved or read.

```ts
class AccountError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Saves an account and queues the event carrying it to the server.

Both in one transaction (ADR-014). An account the till can take money into
and the server has never heard of would have every payment naming it
rejected.

Returns whether the write took effect; it can lose to a later edit from
another device, which is not a failure.
Application adapters supply a native UUIDv7 event ID; omitted IDs retain the
legacy randomUUID behavior for existing repository callers.

```ts
saveAccount(database: Database, account: SaveAccount, eventId?: string): boolean;
```

Writes an account, keeping whichever edit wins.

Shared by a local save and by an edit arriving from another device.

```ts
upsertAccount(tx: Queryable, account: Account, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

Returns an account, or undefined if this device does not hold it.

```ts
accountOf(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, id: string): Account;
```

Lists the accounts a till can take money into, by name.

Closed accounts are absent by default but never deleted: the payments they
took still name them, and history is not deleted (§40).

```ts
listAccounts(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, options: {
    readonly includeClosed?: boolean;
} = {}): Account[];
```

## [packages/core/src/data/finance/cash.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/finance/cash.ts)

The event a cash movement queues.

```ts
const CASH_MOVED: "CASH_MOVED";
```

Why money entered or left an account.

```ts
export type MovementType = "OPENING_BALANCE" | "CASH_IN" | "CASH_OUT" | "TRANSFER_IN" | "TRANSFER_OUT" | "ADJUSTMENT";
```

Money entering or leaving an account by someone's hand.

```ts
export interface CashMovement {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly accountId: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly type: MovementType;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
    readonly counterpartId?: string | undefined;
    readonly reason?: string | undefined;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly recordedAt: string;
}
```

What a caller supplies when recording movements.

```ts
export interface MoveCash {
    readonly movements: readonly CashMovement[];
    readonly eventId: string;
}
```

What an account holds, worked out from what moved through it.

```ts
export interface AccountBalance {
    readonly onHandMinor: number;
    readonly movements: number;
}
```

A cash count set against what the system believes (§18).

```ts
export interface Reconciliation {
    readonly expectedMinor: number;
    readonly countedMinor: number;
    readonly differenceMinor: number;
}
```

Raised when money cannot be moved or read.

```ts
class CashError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Records money moving, and queues the event carrying it.

Several movements at once, because a transfer is two: money leaving one
account and arriving in another. They are written in one transaction, since
half a transfer is money genuinely lost, gone from the till with nothing to
show where it went.

```ts
moveCash(database: Database, request: MoveCash): void;
```

Builds the two halves of moving money between two accounts.

The amount is given as what leaves, negative, exactly as the domain requires
on the server: flipping a positive one silently would be the only place a
sign that contradicts its meaning is corrected rather than refused.

```ts
transfer(leaving: Omit<CashMovement, "type" | "counterpartId">, arriving: Omit<CashMovement, "type" | "counterpartId" | "amountMinor">): CashMovement[];
```

What an account holds: the sum of every source that moved money.

Five arms, because each source is authoritative for its own facts: the
payments taken into the account, the customers settling debts into it, the
movements made by hand, the expenses paid out of it, and the wholesalers
settled out of it.

The signs are where the care is, because a ledger's sign means "more or less
owed" and an account's means "more or less cash", and the two agree only
sometimes. A customer's payment is negative in their ledger and is **negated**
here, where the meaning becomes money arriving. A supplier's payment is
negative in theirs too and is taken **as stored**, because the money really
did leave. Getting that one backwards makes paying a wholesaler look like
taking money in.

A new source of money must be added to this query or the drawer will read
wrong by exactly what it moved.

```ts
balanceOfAccount(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, accountId: string): AccountBalance;
```

An account's movements, oldest first by when the money actually moved.

```ts
movementsOfAccount(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, accountId: string): CashMovement[];
```

Sets a count against what the system believes (§18).

The difference is counted less expected, so a shortfall reads negative: the
direction an adjustment would have to move the account to make the books
agree with the drawer. Nothing is changed here: correcting it is a movement,
which is a new record of what was found rather than an edit to what was
believed.

```ts
reconcile(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, accountId: string, countedMinor: number): Reconciliation;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/finance/terms.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/finance/terms.ts)

Payment terms and the due dates they imply (ADR-033).

Terms are state: last write wins by `(updated_at, updated_by_device)`, like customer details. Due dates are never
stored; they are derived from the append-only ledgers, so changing terms re-ages history without editing it.

Whose debts the terms govern: what customers owe the shop, or what the shop owes suppliers.

```ts
export type TermsParty = "CUSTOMER" | "SUPPLIER";
```

The stored row key: the party's own ID, or `<business>:<party>` for the shop default.

```ts
paymentTermsKey(businessId: string, party: TermsParty, partyId?: string): string;
```

One terms record as written by `PAYMENT_TERMS_SET`. `days` null on a party means "use the shop default".

```ts
export interface PaymentTermsRecord {
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly party: TermsParty;
    readonly partyId?: string | undefined;
    readonly days: number | null;
    readonly updatedAt: string;
}
```

Writes terms under last-write-wins, inside the caller's transaction. Returns false when a newer record is already
stored, so a losing local or replayed write changes nothing.

```ts
writePaymentTerms(tx: Queryable, record: PaymentTermsRecord, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

Terms in force for one party, and where they come from.

```ts
export interface EffectiveTerms {
    readonly days: number;
    readonly source: "party" | "shop" | "default";
    readonly partyDays?: number | undefined;
    readonly partyVersion?: string | undefined;
}
```

The shop default for a party kind, with the stored version when there is one.

```ts
shopTerms(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, party: TermsParty): { readonly days: number; readonly stored: boolean; readonly version?: string; };
```

Terms in force for one customer or supplier.

```ts
termsFor(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, party: TermsParty, partyId: string): EffectiveTerms;
```

The aging of one party's ledger under its current terms.

```ts
agingOf(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, party: TermsParty, partyId: string, now: string): DebtAging & { readonly termsDays: number; };
```

Every party of a kind that owes (or is owed) a positive balance, aged under its terms, highest overdue first.

```ts
agedParties(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, party: TermsParty, now: string): readonly (DebtAging & { readonly partyId: string; readonly name: string; readonly termsDays: number; })[];
```

## [packages/core/src/data/inventory/movements.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/inventory/movements.ts)

Why stock moved (§7).

```ts
export type MovementType = "PURCHASE" | "SALE" | "SALE_RETURN" | "PURCHASE_RETURN" | "DAMAGE" | "ADJUSTMENT" | "STOCK_COUNT" | "TRANSFER_OUT" | "TRANSFER_IN" | "OPENING_BALANCE";
```

What caused a movement.

```ts
export interface MovementReference {
    readonly kind: string;
    readonly id: string;
}
```

A movement to record.

```ts
export interface NewMovement {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly type: MovementType;
    readonly quantity: number;
    readonly unitCostMinor?: number | undefined;
    readonly unitCostCurrency?: string | undefined;
    readonly reference?: MovementReference | undefined;
    readonly reason?: string | undefined;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly recordedAt: string;
}
```

A movement as stored.

```ts
export interface StoredMovement extends NewMovement {
    readonly branchId: string | undefined;
    readonly unitCostMinor: number | undefined;
    readonly unitCostCurrency: string | undefined;
    readonly reference: MovementReference | undefined;
    readonly reason: string | undefined;
}
```

A product's stock, derived from its ledger.

```ts
export interface StockLevel {
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly onHandThousandths: number;
    readonly movements: number;
    readonly lastMovedAt: string | undefined;
}
```

Whether more has left than ever arrived.

Not corrupt data: two devices each selling the last unit offline is expected
(§29), and the honest total is below zero until someone counts the shelf.
A caller surfaces it rather than clamping it, because clamping would hide the
discrepancy the owner needs to see.

```ts
isNegative(level: StockLevel): boolean;
```

Raised when a movement cannot be recorded.

```ts
class MovementError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

How the caller describes the event to queue alongside a movement.

```ts
export interface MovementEvent {
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly type: string;
    readonly context?: Record<string, unknown> | undefined;
}
```

Records a movement and queues the event that will carry it to the server.

Both happen in one transaction. If either fails, neither happened, which is
the only way to make it impossible for a device to hold stock history the
server will never hear about (ADR-014).

```ts
recordMovement(database: Database, movement: NewMovement, event: MovementEvent): void;
```

Records several movements and one event covering them all.

A business event fans out: a sale of three lines moves three products' stock,
and those movements are one fact. They are queued as one event because the
server applies the sale, not three unrelated stock changes, and because a
partial arrival would leave the server with a sale that does not balance.

```ts
recordMovements(database: Database, movements: readonly NewMovement[], event: MovementEvent): void;
```

Returns a product's stock, summed by the database.

A product sold for a year has thousands of movements, and a till asking how
many are left must not load them all (§46). The answer is the same addition
either way.

With `branchId`, only that branch's movements count: what a count taken on
that branch's shelves is compared against. Without it, the whole business.

```ts
stockOf(database: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string, branchId?: string): StockLevel;
```

Returns what a product's stock was at a given moment.

It counts by when stock moved, not when this device heard about it, so a
movement synced days late still lands in the period it belongs to.

```ts
stockAsOf(database: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string, asOf: string): StockLevel;
```

Returns a product's movements, oldest first by when the stock moved.

```ts
movementsFor(database: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string): StoredMovement[];
```

Shop catalogue grouping; details use the same timestamp/device ordering as products.

```ts
export interface Category {

    readonly active: boolean;
    readonly createdAt: string;
    readonly updatedAt: string;
    readonly updatedByDevice?: string | undefined;
}
```

Returns a category only within the requested shop.

```ts
categoryOf(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, id: string): Category;
```

Shared category LWW primitive for authorized writes and verified replay, inside their transaction.

```ts
upsertCategory(tx: Queryable, item: Category, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/products/photos.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/products/photos.ts)

Size limits for product photos.

```ts
const PHOTO_LIMITS: { readonly maxBytes: number; readonly maxSide: 512; readonly shopBytes: number; };
```

The encodings a photo may be stored in.

```ts
export type PhotoMediaType = "image/webp" | "image/jpeg";
```

Raised when a photo is not a usable image or would not fit.

```ts
class PhotoError extends Error {
  code: "invalid_photo" | "photo_storage_full";
  constructor(code: PhotoError["code"]);
}
```

A stored product photo.

```ts
export interface ProductPhoto {
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly digest: string;
    readonly mediaType: PhotoMediaType;
    readonly bytes: Uint8Array;
    readonly width: number;
    readonly height: number;
    readonly updatedAt: string;
}
```

What a caller supplies to store a photo; dimensions and type come from .

```ts
export interface WritePhoto {
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly digest: string;
    readonly bytes: Uint8Array;
    readonly at: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
}
```

Reads the type and size of an encoded image from its header, refusing anything
that is not a WebP or JPEG within the limits. Pixels are not decoded here.

```ts
inspectPhoto(bytes: Uint8Array): { mediaType: PhotoMediaType; width: number; height: number; };
```

Stores or replaces a product's photo, refusing one that would take the shop
past `shopBytes` (by default ). The replaced photo does not count.

```ts
writeProductPhoto(tx: Queryable, photo: WritePhoto, shopBytes: number = PHOTO_LIMITS.shopBytes): void;
```

Removes a product's photo; returns whether there was one.

```ts
deleteProductPhoto(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string): boolean;
```

A product's photo, or undefined when it has none on this device.

```ts
productPhoto(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string): ProductPhoto;
```

A product's photo digest without its bytes, for listing and cache keys.

```ts
productPhotoDigest(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string): string;
```

How many photos the shop holds and their total size.

```ts
photoUsage(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, shopBytes: number = PHOTO_LIMITS.shopBytes): { photos: number; bytes: number; limitBytes: number; };
```

## [packages/core/src/data/products/product-categories.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/products/product-categories.ts)

A product's additional categories as last written, with the version that wrote them.

```ts
export interface ProductCategorySet {
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly categoryIds: readonly string[];
    readonly updatedAt: string;
}
```

Replaces a product's additional categories under last-write-wins (later `updatedAt`, then the
greater device ID), the rule product details use. Returns false when a newer set is already
stored, so a losing local or replayed write changes nothing. Inside the caller's transaction.

```ts
writeProductCategories(tx: Queryable, set: ProductCategorySet, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

A product's additional category IDs and their version, or an empty set never written.

```ts
productCategorySet(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string): { categoryIds: string[]; updatedAt?: string; };
```

## [packages/core/src/data/products/products.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/products/products.ts)

The event a product write queues.

```ts
const PRODUCT_SAVED: "PRODUCT_SAVED";
```

A product as the device holds it.

```ts
export interface Product {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly categoryId?: string | undefined;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly sku?: string | undefined;
    readonly barcode?: string | undefined;
    readonly description?: string | undefined;
    readonly brand?: string | undefined;
    readonly unit: string;
    readonly divisible: boolean;
    readonly purchasePriceMinor?: number | undefined;
    readonly sellingPriceMinor: number;
    readonly currency: string;
    readonly taxRateBasisPoints: number;
    readonly minimumStock: number;
    readonly active: boolean;
    readonly createdAt: string;
    readonly updatedAt: string;
    readonly updatedByDevice?: string | undefined;
}
```

What a caller supplies when saving a product.

```ts
export interface SaveProduct extends Product {
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
}
```

Raised when a product cannot be saved or read.

```ts
class ProductError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Saves a product and queues the event carrying it to the server.

Both in one transaction: a product the shopkeeper can see and the server
never will is the loss ADR-014 exists to prevent, and it applies to a price
correction as much as to a sale.

Returns whether the write took effect. A local save normally does (the
shopkeeper is editing what is in front of them), but it can lose to an edit
from another device that arrived with a later timestamp, and the caller
should show what is actually stored rather than what was typed.
Application adapters supply a native UUIDv7 event ID; omitted IDs retain the
legacy randomUUID behavior for existing repository callers.

```ts
saveProduct(database: Database, product: SaveProduct, eventId?: string): boolean;
```

Writes a product, keeping whichever edit wins.

Shared by a local save and by an edit arriving from another device, so both
are resolved by the same comparison.

```ts
upsert(tx: Queryable, product: Product, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

Returns a product, or undefined if this device does not hold it.

```ts
productOf(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, id: string): Product;
```

Finds a product by its barcode, which is the POS path.

A code identifies a product within one business, not globally: two shops may
each sell the same manufacturer's barcode.

```ts
productByBarcode(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, barcode: string): Product;
```

Finds products by what a cashier types, best match first (§46).

The POS path, and the one §46 names as having to be fast. A cashier types
three or four letters between one customer and the next, so this has to be a
prefix match an index can serve rather than a scan, and it has to rank,
because the first row is the one that gets tapped.

The ranking is deliberate and in this order:

1. An exact barcode. A scanner types the whole code and presses enter, and
   when that code is a product there is nothing to choose between.
2. An exact SKU, for the shops that use one at the counter.
3. A name starting with the term. "sav" finds Savon before Maggi Savoureux,
   because a person typing three letters is spelling the start of a word.
4. A name containing it anywhere, which is what finds "Lait" in "Nido Lait".

Archived products are absent: a till offering something the shop no longer
sells wastes the one tap the cashier had.

`LIKE` with a leading wildcard cannot use an index, and that is accepted for
arm 4 only: a shop's catalogue is thousands of rows, not millions, and the
arms above it are the ones that answer a scanner. If a catalogue ever grows
past what this serves in a keystroke, FTS5 is the next step and this function
is where it goes.

```ts
searchProducts(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, term: string, limit = 20): Product[];
```

Lists the products a business sells, by name.

```ts
listProducts(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, options: {
    readonly includeArchived?: boolean;
} = {}): Product[];
```

## [packages/core/src/data/products/tax.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/products/tax.ts)

Tax settings and product tax rules (ADR-033). Prices include tax; these records only decide which rate a sale line
carries, frozen on the line when it sells. Both are last-write-wins state by `(updated_at, updated_by_device)`.

The shop's tax settings.

```ts
export interface TaxSettings {
    readonly enabled: boolean;
    readonly label: string;
    readonly standardRateBasisPoints: number;
    readonly registration?: string | undefined;
    readonly updatedAt?: string | undefined;
}
```

What a shop that has never saved its tax settings gets: tax off, Cameroon's standard rate ready to switch on.

```ts
const DEFAULT_TAX_SETTINGS: TaxSettings;
```

A product's tax: the shop's standard rate, none, or its own rate.

```ts
export type ProductTaxRule = {
    readonly rule: "STANDARD" | "EXEMPT";
    readonly updatedAt?: string | undefined;
} | {
    readonly rule: "RATE";
    readonly rateBasisPoints: number;
    readonly updatedAt?: string | undefined;
};
```

The shop's settings, or the defaults when none were saved.

```ts
taxSettings(tx: Queryable, businessId: string): TaxSettings;
```

Writes the settings under last-write-wins inside the caller's transaction; false when a newer version is stored.

```ts
writeTaxSettings(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, settings: TaxSettings & {
    readonly updatedAt: string;
}, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

A product's rule. With none stored it follows the standard rate, except that a non-zero legacy
`products.tax_rate_basis_points` (never written by current apps) counts as its own rate.

```ts
productTaxRule(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string): ProductTaxRule;
```

Writes a product's rule under last-write-wins inside the caller's transaction; false when a newer rule is stored.

```ts
writeProductTaxRule(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string, rule: ProductTaxRule & {
    readonly updatedAt: string;
}, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

The rate a sale line of this product carries now: 0 while tax is off or the product is exempt.

```ts
effectiveTaxRate(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string, settings: TaxSettings = taxSettings(tx, businessId)): number;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/purchases/purchases.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/purchases/purchases.ts)

The event a recorded delivery queues.

```ts
const PURCHASE_RECORDED: "PURCHASE_RECORDED";
```

One line of a delivery.

```ts
export interface DeliveryLine {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly movementId: string;
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly unitCostMinor: number;
    readonly quantity: number;
}
```

Money handed over at the door.

```ts
export interface DoorPayment {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly accountId: string;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
    readonly reason: string;
}
```

A delivery about to be recorded.

```ts
export interface Delivery {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly supplierId?: string | undefined;
    readonly reference?: string | undefined;
    readonly lines: readonly DeliveryLine[];
    readonly payments: readonly DoorPayment[];
    readonly note?: string | undefined;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly recordedAt: string;
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly supplierTransactionId?: string | undefined;
}
```

Where a recorded delivery stands.

```ts
export interface DeliveryTotals {
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly paidMinor: number;
    readonly outstandingMinor: number;
}
```

Raised when a delivery cannot be recorded.

```ts
class PurchaseError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Records a delivery: the purchase, its lines, the stock it brought in, the
money handed over, the debt for the rest, and the event carrying all of it.

Returns the totals, so a screen can show what was actually recorded rather
than what it worked out separately; two answers to the same arithmetic is how
a delivery note comes to disagree with a report.

```ts
recordPurchase(database: Database, delivery: Delivery): DeliveryTotals;
```

Works out what a delivery comes to, refusing anything that does not add up.

Exported because a screen needs the running total before anything is written,
and it must be this function, not a second one in the interface. Two
implementations of the same arithmetic is how the screen comes to say one
number and the delivery note another.

```ts
totalsOfDelivery(delivery: Delivery): DeliveryTotals;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/reports/analytics.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/reports/analytics.ts)

How a trend is sliced. Weeks start on Monday.

```ts
export type Bucket = "hour" | "day" | "week" | "month";
```

One slice of a sales trend.

```ts
export interface SalesPoint {
    readonly key: string;
    readonly revenueMinor: number;
    readonly transactions: number;
}
```

One slice of a profit trend: a sales point with what the goods cost.

```ts
export interface ProfitPoint extends SalesPoint {
    readonly costMinor: number;
    readonly grossProfitMinor: number;
}
```

Sales started in one local hour of one weekday, summed over the period.

```ts
export interface ActivityCell {
    readonly weekday: number;
    readonly hour: number;
    readonly transactions: number;
    readonly revenueMinor: number;
}
```

What one product sold over a period, net of its returns.

```ts
export interface ProductSales {
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly soldThousandths: number;
    readonly revenueMinor: number;
}
```

 with what the goods cost (§8).

```ts
export interface ProductProfit extends ProductSales {
    readonly costMinor: number;
    readonly grossProfitMinor: number;
    readonly linesWithoutCost: number;
}
```

One named customer's buying over a period.

```ts
export interface CustomerRank {
    readonly customerId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly transactions: number;
    readonly revenueMinor: number;
    readonly lastPurchaseAt: string;
}
```

The finest bucket that keeps a period to a readable number of points: hours
for up to two days, days for up to about two months, weeks for up to about a
year, months beyond.

```ts
defaultBucket(from: string, to: string): Bucket;
```

Every bucket key a period touches, in order, including the empty ones a
trend needs to show as zero rather than skip.

```ts
bucketKeys(from: string, to: string, bucket: Bucket, offsetMinutes: number): string[];
```

Takings, cost and sales count per bucket, empty buckets included (§21).

Sale lines, bill discounts and returns are each placed in the bucket in which
they *happened*, which is how `profitBetween` slices a period; summed, the
points equal its revenue, cost and gross profit exactly.

```ts
profitTrend(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string, bucket: Bucket, offsetMinutes: number): ProfitPoint[];
```

 without cost, for those who may see sales but not margins.

```ts
salesTrend(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string, bucket: Bucket, offsetMinutes: number): SalesPoint[];
```

When the shop is busy: sales by local weekday and hour (§22).

Only cells with at least one sale are returned. A sale counts where it was
rung up; its later returns are not activity at the hour they reversed.

```ts
salesActivity(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string, offsetMinutes: number): ActivityCell[];
```

What every product that moved in a period sold and earned, best takings first.

Net of the returns made in the period, product by product, so a product
returned but not sold in the period appears with negative figures rather than
vanishing. Named by the product's current name, so a renamed product is one
row. Revenue is net of each sale's whole-bill discount, allocated across its
lines exactly as a return refunds it, so a fully returned line nets to zero and
the products sum to 's period revenue.

```ts
productProfit(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): ProductProfit[];
```

 without cost, for those who may see sales but not margins.

```ts
productSales(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): ProductSales[];
```

The named customers who bought most over a period, most first (§12, §21).

Every sale naming them counts, paid or on credit. Walk-in sales name nobody
and are absent. Returns made in the period against their sales come off;
a customer who only returned goods in the period bought nothing in it and is
not ranked.

```ts
topCustomers(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string, limit = 10): CustomerRank[];
```

## [packages/core/src/data/reports/reports.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/reports/reports.ts)

What a period sold.

```ts
export interface SalesSummary {
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly transactions: number;
    readonly itemsSoldThousandths: number;
}
```

What a period earned, and what it cost to earn (§8, §20).

```ts
export interface ProfitSummary {
    readonly revenueMinor: number;
    readonly costMinor: number;
    readonly grossProfitMinor: number;
    readonly linesWithoutCost: number;
}
```

What a period's deliveries brought in, and what they cost (§15, §21).

```ts
export interface PurchaseSummary {
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly deliveries: number;
    readonly receivedThousandths: number;
}
```

How stock is valued (§8).

Named rather than inlined because §8 requires the architecture to allow FIFO
and weighted average later. Adding one is a case in , not a
rewrite of the report, and callers name the method they mean, so a screen
that changes it changes a report's meaning visibly rather than by accident.

```ts
export type CostingMethod = "LATEST_COST";
```

What one product's stock is worth.

```ts
export interface ValuedProduct {
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly onHandThousandths: number;
    readonly unitCostMinor?: number | undefined;
    readonly valueMinor?: number | undefined;
}
```

What the shelves are worth (§8, §21).

```ts
export interface Valuation {
    readonly method: CostingMethod;
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly products: readonly ValuedProduct[];
    readonly uncosted: number;
}
```

How a product moved over a period (§21).

```ts
export interface ProductMovement {
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly soldThousandths: number;
    readonly revenueMinor: number;
    readonly onHandThousandths: number;
}
```

Stock that is not selling, and what it has tied up (§21).

```ts
export interface DeadStock {
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly onHandThousandths: number;
    readonly lastSoldAt?: string | undefined;
    readonly valueMinor?: number | undefined;
}
```

What moved through an account over a period (§18, §21).

```ts
export interface CashFlow {
    readonly accountId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly inMinor: number;
    readonly outMinor: number;
    readonly netMinor: number;
    readonly closingMinor: number;
}
```

One entry of a customer's statement (§13, §21).

```ts
export interface StatementEntry {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly type: string;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
    readonly reference?: string | undefined;
    readonly reason?: string | undefined;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly balanceMinor: number;
}
```

One line of a breakdown, largest first.

```ts
export interface Line {
    readonly key: string;
    readonly label: string;
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly count: number;
}
```

§22's dashboard: what an owner looks at first.

```ts
export interface Dashboard {
    readonly sales: SalesSummary;
    readonly profit: ProfitSummary;
    readonly expensesMinor: number;
    readonly netResultMinor: number;
    readonly customerDebtMinor: number;
    readonly supplierDebtMinor: number;
    readonly lowStock: number;
}
```

What a period sold (§21).

```ts
salesBetween(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): SalesSummary;
```

What a period earned against what the goods cost (§8, §20).

Cost is counted only for the lines that recorded one. A line with no cost
contributes its revenue and no cost, and is counted in `linesWithoutCost` so
the figure can be read for what it is: profit over the part of the trade the
shop knows the cost of.

```ts
profitBetween(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): ProfitSummary;
```

What sold, by product, best first (§21).

Net, like every sales breakdown here: after line and whole-bill discounts and
less returns made in the period (see ), so the lines sum to
's total. A product only returned in the period shows a
negative total and a count of zero.

```ts
salesByProduct(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): Line[];
```

What each category sold, best first (§21).

Joined through the *product* rather than stored on the sale line, because a
category is a way of grouping products and moving one between categories has
to move its history with it. A category copied onto the line at the time of
sale would leave last month's report grouping by a shelf the shop no longer
has, the opposite of what a line's *name* and *price* are copied for, which
is that a receipt must show what was actually charged.

Products in no category are grouped under an empty key rather than dropped: a
breakdown of takings that did not add up to the takings would be worse than
an "uncategorised" line. For the same reason the lines are net of discounts
and the period's returns, and sum to 's total.

```ts
salesByCategory(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): Line[];
```

What each account took, best first (§21).

By account and never by "payment method": which provider took the money is
the account's name, which is what makes adding one a row rather than a
release (§10).

Net of sale refunds paid out of each account in the period; `count` is the
number of sale payments. Credit left owing reaches no account, so these sum to
the period's sales only when nothing was sold on credit.

```ts
salesByAccount(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): Line[];
```

Who sold what, best first: §21's sales by employee.

Net of discounts; a return counts against the cashier who made the original
sale, in the period it happened. `count` is distinct sales. Sums to
's total.

```ts
salesByUser(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): Line[];
```

What every customer owes, most first (§13, §21).

Customers in credit are absent: they are not debts, and a list of debts that
included them would have to be read twice to be understood.

```ts
outstandingDebt(tx: Queryable, businessId: string): Line[];
```

What the shop owes every wholesaler, most first (§16, §21).

The mirror of , and deliberately a separate query over
a separate table. One list with a sign would put a customer who owes 5,000 and
a wholesaler owed 5,000 on the same line of the same report, and they are
opposite obligations; netting them would tell an owner the shop was square
when it was owed money by one person and owed money to another.

Wholesalers the shop has paid ahead are absent, for the reason customers in
credit are: they are not debts, and a list of debts that included them would
have to be read twice to be understood.

```ts
supplierDebt(tx: Queryable, businessId: string): Line[];
```

What a period bought, by wholesaler (§21's purchase history).

Sliced by when the stock *arrived*, like every period here, so a delivery
recorded on a phone that synced on Friday still lands in Monday.

A delivery that named nobody (a crate from the market) is grouped under an
empty key rather than dropped. What the shop spent on stock is the question,
and leaving those out would answer a different one.

Less the value of purchase returns made in the period, against the original
delivery's wholesaler, so the lines sum to 's total.
`count` is deliveries received in the period.

```ts
purchasesBySupplier(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): Line[];
```

What a period's deliveries cost in total (§21).

```ts
purchasesBetween(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): PurchaseSummary;
```

What a period paid its wholesalers, by wholesaler (§21).

Reported positive, though the ledger stores a payment negative: "paid 50,000"
is what an owner is asking, and a column of negative numbers under the
heading "supplier payments" reads as money coming back.

Money handed over at the door is deliberately absent. That is an
`account_movements` row against the till, not a ledger entry, because a
delivery from the market has no wholesaler to have a ledger; it shows in the
period's purchases instead, which is where the goods it bought are.

```ts
supplierPaymentsBetween(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): Line[];
```

What one unit of a product costs now under `method`, or undefined when nothing says.

A sale freezes this on its lines at checkout, so the profit it reports and the
valuation of the stock left behind read the same cost (§8); returns and later
reports keep the frozen figure.

```ts
currentUnitCost(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, productId: string, method: CostingMethod = "LATEST_COST"): number;
```

What the stock on the shelves is worth (§8, §21).

Quantity is the sum of the ledger, as everywhere; the cost comes from
. Nothing here is a stored total, which is what makes the
valuation and the deliveries behind it incapable of disagreeing.

Products holding nothing are absent: a shop that has ever sold out of
something would otherwise carry it forever in a report about what it owns.
Products holding *less* than nothing are **present, at a negative value**:
two devices each selling the last unit offline is expected (§29), and the
honest total is below zero until someone counts the shelf. Hiding it would
make the one figure that reveals the problem the one figure that conceals it.

```ts
inventoryValuation(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, method: CostingMethod = "LATEST_COST"): Valuation;
```

How every product moved over a period, best first (§21).

A LEFT JOIN from products rather than a GROUP BY over sale lines, because the
products that sold **nothing** are the whole point of the slow end of this
list and a join the other way would leave them out. That is the difference
between "these sold least" and "these did not sell", and only the second is
worth a shopkeeper's attention.

Ranked by quantity rather than by takings: a cheap thing that shifts every day
is fast-moving even though a single generator outsells a year of it. What it
earned is reported alongside, so a caller can see both without a second query.

Both are net of the period's returns, and takings are net of whole-bill
discounts, so a sale that came back does not make a product look fast.

```ts
productMovement(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): ProductMovement[];
```

What shifted most over a period (§21).

```ts
fastMoving(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string, limit = 10): ProductMovement[];
```

What shifted least over a period, worst first (§21).

The same ranking read from the other end, deliberately, so the two lists
cannot disagree about where a product sits.

```ts
slowMoving(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string, limit = 10): ProductMovement[];
```

Stock that has not sold since a date, and what it has tied up (§21).

The actionable version of the slow end: a shopkeeper does not need to know
that something sold twice instead of thirty times, they need to know which
shelves are holding money that is not moving. So this is stock **on hand**
that sold **nothing** in the period; a product that sold out is not dead, it
is finished, and one nobody stocks is not dead either.

The value is at latest cost, and absent when nothing says what it
cost: the same honest gap a valuation reports rather than a zero that would
make dead stock look free to keep.

```ts
deadStock(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, since: string, method: CostingMethod = "LATEST_COST"): DeadStock[];
```

What went in and out of each account over a period (§21).

In and out are reported apart rather than netted, because "took 400,000 and
paid out 380,000" and "took 20,000" are different days and only the first is
the truth about a busy one.

The closing figure is the whole history, not the period: an owner comparing a
report against the drawer is asking what is in it now, and a period-only
figure would disagree with §18's count for a reason nobody could see.

Accounts nothing has ever moved through are absent. An account opened and
never used has no flow to report, and a row of zeroes would read as one that
had been emptied.

```ts
cashFlow(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): CashFlow[];
```

A customer's statement: every entry, oldest first, with a running balance
(§13, §21's payment history).

Ordered by when things **happened**, not when they arrived, so an entry synced
days late lands where it belongs and the running balance reads as the customer
experienced it. That is why the balance is worked out in order here rather
than by a window function over arrival: a statement whose lines are right but
whose running total is in arrival order would be a document that adds up and
still cannot be read aloud.

The whole ledger, not a period. A statement covering last month that opened at
zero would claim a debt began then, and the argument this is for is about what
is owed in total.

```ts
customerStatement(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, customerId: string): StatementEntry[];
```

What a customer bought over a period, by product (§21's purchase history).

Every sale to them, not only the ones on credit: "what does this person buy"
is a question about a regular, and answering it from the credit ledger alone
would describe only the days they happened to be short.

Net of discounts and of the returns they made in the period.

```ts
customerPurchases(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, customerId: string, from: string, to: string): Line[];
```

The products at or below their reorder point (§7, §21).

Stock is the sum of the ledger, here as everywhere. Products with a reorder
point of zero are not tracked and are absent, rather than every product a
shop has ever sold out of appearing forever.

```ts
lowStock(tx: Queryable, businessId: string): Line[];
```

§22's dashboard, assembled from the reports above.

What is owed *to* the shop and what is owed *by* it are two figures, never one
netted number. An owner with 80,000 out on credit and 80,000 owed to a
wholesaler is not square: one of those arrives when customers choose to pay
and the other falls due when the van comes back.

```ts
dashboard(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): Dashboard;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/reports/tax.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/reports/tax.ts)

A period's tax-inclusive sales by rate (ADR-033), with the totals across rates.

```ts
export interface TaxSummary {
    readonly groups: readonly TaxGroup[];
    readonly grossMinor: number;
    readonly taxMinor: number;
    readonly netMinor: number;
}
```

Tax collected over `[from, to)` by the rate frozen on each sale line, net of sale returns made in the period.

Bill discounts are spread over a sale's lines by the deterministic allocation returns use, and returns count at the
amount refunded or credited for the line, so the gross column adds up to the period's net sales. Tax is rounded once
per rate. Rate 0 (untaxed or exempt) is kept as its own group.

```ts
taxBetween(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, from: string, to: string): TaxSummary;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/returns/returns.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/returns/returns.ts)

A return is serialized per original transaction through an immutable predecessor link.

```ts
export type ReturnKind = "SALE" | "PURCHASE";
```

Requested original item quantities in thousandths.

```ts
export interface ReturnSelection {
    readonly originalItemId: string;
    readonly quantity: number;
}
```

Source facts and currently remaining quantities; mutable product prices are never used.

```ts
export interface ReturnSource {
    readonly originalId: string;
    readonly priorId?: string | undefined;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly counterpartyId?: string | undefined;
    readonly refundableMinor: number;
    readonly items: readonly {
        id: string;
        productId: string;
        name: string;
        originalQuantity: number;
        returnedQuantity: number;
        netMinor: number;
        unitCostMinor?: number | undefined;
    }[];
}
```

Reads immutable source/return facts within the caller's scoped transaction; missing prerequisites are retriable.
Remaining quantities and refunds count every return of the original, or with `basis` only the returns that one was
signed against (`null`: none), so every till computes a received return exactly as the till that made it did.

```ts
returnSource(tx: Queryable, shop: string, kind: ReturnKind, originalId: string, basis?: string | null): ReturnSource;
```

Exact cumulative entitlement and stock quantities for a partial/full return; over-return is refused.

```ts
planReturn(source: ReturnSource, selection: readonly ReturnSelection[]): { totalMinor: number; costMinor: number; lines: readonly { originalItemId: string; productId: string; quantity: number; amountMinor: number; costMinor: number; }[]; };
```

Bounded return list for a source transaction; shared by receipt/purchase presentation.

```ts
returnsFor(tx: Queryable, shop: string, kind: ReturnKind, id: string): Row[];
```

## [packages/core/src/data/sales/sales.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/sales/sales.ts)

The event a completed sale queues.

```ts
const SALE_COMPLETED: "SALE_COMPLETED";
```

One line of a cart.

```ts
export interface CartLine {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly movementId: string;
    readonly productId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly unitPriceMinor: number;
    readonly quantity: number;
    readonly discountMinor?: number | undefined;
    readonly taxRateBasisPoints?: number | undefined;
    readonly unitCostMinor?: number | undefined;
}
```

One payment tendered against a sale.

```ts
export interface CartPayment {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly accountId: string;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
    readonly reference?: string | undefined;
    readonly note?: string | undefined;
}
```

A sale about to be completed.

```ts
export interface Cart {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly customerId?: string | undefined;
    readonly number: string;
    readonly lines: readonly CartLine[];
    readonly payments: readonly CartPayment[];
    readonly discountMinor?: number | undefined;
    readonly note?: string | undefined;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly recordedAt: string;
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly creditTransactionId?: string | undefined;
}
```

Where a completed sale stands.

```ts
export interface SaleTotals {
    readonly subtotalMinor: number;
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly paidMinor: number;
    readonly outstandingMinor: number;
}
```

Raised when a sale cannot be completed.

```ts
class SaleError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Completes a sale: writes it, its lines, its stock movements, its payments,
and the event that carries them, all in one transaction.

Returns the totals, so a till can show what was actually recorded rather than
what it worked out separately; two answers to the same arithmetic is how a
receipt comes to disagree with a report.

```ts
completeSale(database: Database, cart: Cart): SaleTotals;
```

Works out what a sale comes to, refusing anything that does not add up.

Exported because a till needs the running total of a cart before anything is
written, and it must be this function, not a second one in the interface.
Two implementations of the same arithmetic is how the screen comes to say one
number and the receipt another.

```ts
totalsOf(cart: Cart): SaleTotals;
```

The sum of the lines, before the sale's own discount.

Exported because the applier that receives a sale from another device has to
work out what it left owing, and must reach that number by this arithmetic.
A second implementation is how one till comes to think a customer owes 8,000
and another 8,001.

```ts
subtotalOf(lines: readonly PricedLine[]): number;
```

What is needed to price a line: the rest of a CartLine is for the record.

```ts
export interface PricedLine {
    readonly unitPriceMinor: number;
    readonly quantity: number;
    readonly discountMinor?: number | undefined;
}
```

## [packages/core/src/data/suppliers/suppliers.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/suppliers/suppliers.ts)

The event a supplier write queues.

```ts
const SUPPLIER_SAVED: "SUPPLIER_SAVED";
```

The event a payment settling a wholesaler's account queues.

```ts
const SUPPLIER_PAYMENT: "SUPPLIER_PAYMENT";
```

A wholesaler as the device holds them.

```ts
export interface Supplier {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly phone?: string | undefined;
    readonly address?: string | undefined;
    readonly contact?: string | undefined;
    readonly notes?: string | undefined;
    readonly active: boolean;
    readonly createdAt: string;
    readonly updatedAt: string;
    readonly updatedByDevice?: string | undefined;
}
```

What a caller supplies when saving a supplier.

```ts
export interface SaveSupplier extends Supplier {
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
}
```

What the business owes a wholesaler, worked out from their ledger.

```ts
export interface SupplierBalance {
    readonly outstandingMinor: number;
    readonly entries: number;
}
```

Raised when a supplier cannot be saved or read.

```ts
class SupplierError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Saves a wholesaler and queues the event carrying them to the server.

Both in one transaction (ADR-014). A supplier the shopkeeper can see and the
server never will is worse than a product: a delivery recorded against them
would be refused everywhere else in the shop.

Returns whether the write took effect. A local save normally does, but it can
lose to an edit from another device that arrived with a later timestamp, and
the caller should show what is stored rather than what was typed.
Application adapters supply a native UUIDv7 event ID; omitted IDs retain the
legacy randomUUID behavior for existing repository callers.

```ts
saveSupplier(database: Database, supplier: SaveSupplier, eventId?: string): boolean;
```

Writes a supplier, keeping whichever edit wins.

Shared by a local save and by an edit arriving from another device, so both
are resolved by the same comparison.

```ts
upsertSupplier(tx: Queryable, supplier: Supplier, deviceId: string): boolean;
```

The business settling some or all of a wholesaler's bill.

```ts
export interface SupplierPayment {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly supplierId: string;
    readonly accountId: string;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
    readonly note?: string | undefined;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly recordedAt: string;
    readonly eventId: string;
}
```

Records the business paying off some or all of what it owes a wholesaler.

The entry and the event that carries it in one transaction (ADR-014), like
every other write here: a payment the shopkeeper has made and the server never
hears of would leave the shop owing money it has already handed over.

Payments are against the account, not against a delivery. A wholesaler's bill
is built from a month of deliveries, and asking which of them the 50,000 pays
off would be inventing a question the shop does not ask.

Nothing is checked against the balance. Paying more than is owed leaves the
business in credit, which is the honest answer; refusing it would refuse a
payment the shopkeeper has actually made.

```ts
recordSupplierPayment(database: Database, payment: SupplierPayment): void;
```

Returns a supplier, or undefined if this device does not hold them.

```ts
supplierOf(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, id: string): Supplier;
```

Lists a business's suppliers, by name.

```ts
listSuppliers(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, options: {
    readonly includeInactive?: boolean;
} = {}): Supplier[];
```

What the business owes a wholesaler: the sum of the ledger, never a stored
number (§16).

Summed by SQLite rather than by loading every entry, because a shop that has
bought from one wholesaler for two years has hundreds and a screen asking what
it owes must not pay for all of them (§46).

```ts
balanceOfSupplier(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, supplierId: string): SupplierBalance;
```

A wholesaler's ledger, oldest first by when things happened.

```ts
ledgerOfSupplier(tx: Queryable, businessId: string, supplierId: string): Row[];
```

## [packages/core/src/data/sync/engine.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/sync/engine.ts)

What one applier can do with a received event.

```ts
export interface EventApplier {
    readonly types: readonly string[];
    apply(tx: Queryable, event: InboxEvent): void;
}
```

Thrown by an applier for an event that will never apply, however long it
waits: a payload it cannot read, a shape it does not recognise.

```ts
class UnusableEvent extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

What a sync run did.

```ts
export interface SyncOutcome {
    readonly pushed: number;
    readonly duplicates: number;
    readonly rejected: number;
    readonly stillPending: number;
    readonly received: number;
    readonly applied: number;
    readonly stillDeferred: number;
    readonly unusable: number;
    readonly cursor: number;
    readonly interrupted: string | null;
}
```

How the engine is set up.

```ts
export interface EngineOptions {
    readonly database: Database;
    readonly transport: SyncTransport;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly appliers?: readonly EventApplier[];
    readonly pushBatchSize?: number;
    readonly pullBatchSize?: number;
    readonly maxRoundTrips?: number;
    readonly now?: () => Date;
}
```

How many events one pull asks for by default.

```ts
const DEFAULT_PULL_BATCH_SIZE: 200;
```

How many round trips one run makes before yielding.

```ts
const DEFAULT_MAX_ROUND_TRIPS: 20;
```

Drains the outbox and the inbox.

One engine per database. `sync()` is not safe to run concurrently with itself
(see the guard in .

```ts
class SyncEngine {
  constructor(options: EngineOptions);
  /** Sends what is queued and applies what has arrived.
   * 
   * It does not throw for an unreachable server. Losing the network is ordinary
   * here, and a caller that had to catch it would end up treating the ordinary
   * case as an error. */
  sync(): Promise<SyncOutcome>;
}
```

```ts
const EXPENSE_RECORDED: "EXPENSE_RECORDED";
```

```ts
const expenseApplier: EventApplier;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/sync/inbox.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/sync/inbox.ts)

How far along a received event is.

```ts
export type InboxState = "deferred" | "applied" | "unusable";
```

An event received from the server.

```ts
export interface InboxEvent {
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly type: string;
    readonly payload: unknown;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly serverSequence: number;
    readonly state: InboxState;
    readonly attempts: number;
    readonly lastError: string | null;
}
```

What the caller supplies when an event arrives.

```ts
export interface ReceivedEvent {
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly type: string;
    readonly payload: unknown;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly serverSequence: number;
}
```

Raised when a received event cannot be stored or read.

```ts
class InboxError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Stores an event that arrived, reporting whether it was new.

An event already here is left exactly as it is. A pull that is retried after a
lost response delivers the same events again, and re-storing one would reset
an event already applied back to deferred, applying it a second time.

```ts
receive(tx: Queryable, event: ReceivedEvent, at: string): boolean;
```

Returns events still waiting to be applied, in the server's order.

Server order matters here in a way device order does not: it is the one
ordering every device agrees on.

```ts
deferred(tx: Queryable, limit: number): InboxEvent[];
```

How many received events are still waiting to be applied.

```ts
deferredCount(tx: Queryable): number;
```

Marks an event as applied to this device's tables.

```ts
markApplied(tx: Queryable, eventId: string, at: string): boolean;
```

Records that applying failed, leaving the event deferred so it is tried again.

This is the ordinary case for an event that arrived before what it depends on.
It is not an error to report to anyone yet; the next sync usually resolves it.

```ts
markDeferred(tx: Queryable, eventId: string, reason: string): boolean;
```

Sets an event aside as one this device cannot use.

For an event that will never apply however long it waits: a type this build
does not know, or a payload it cannot read. It is kept rather than deleted:
it is another device's business record, and a build that does understand it
may yet be installed.

```ts
markUnusable(tx: Queryable, eventId: string, reason: string): boolean;
```

Returns events this device could not use, for someone to look at.

```ts
unusable(tx: Queryable): InboxEvent[];
```

Where the next pull should resume from.

Zero means the beginning, which is what a device that has never synced wants.

```ts
cursor(tx: Queryable): number;
```

Moves the cursor forward.

It only ever moves forward. An out-of-order or repeated answer must not walk
it backwards, which would re-deliver events this device already holds.

```ts
advanceCursor(tx: Queryable, to: number, at: string): number;
```

When this device last heard from the server, or null if it never has.

```ts
lastPulledAt(tx: Queryable): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/sync/outbox.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/sync/outbox.ts)

An event waiting to be sent, or already sent.

```ts
export interface OutboxEvent {
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly type: string;
    readonly payload: unknown;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly sequence: number;
    readonly state: DeliveryState;
    readonly attempts: number;
    readonly lastError: string | null;
    readonly deliveredAt: string | null;
}
```

How far along an event is.

```ts
export type DeliveryState = "pending" | "delivered" | "rejected";
```

What the caller supplies when queueing an event.

```ts
export interface NewEvent {
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly type: string;
    readonly payload: unknown;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
}
```

Raised when an event cannot be queued or read.

```ts
class OutboxError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
});
}
```

Appends an event to the outbox.

It takes a  rather than a Database precisely so it cannot be
called on its own: the caller must already be inside the transaction that is
writing the business rows. That is the whole point (see ADR-014).

```ts
enqueue(tx: Queryable, event: NewEvent): OutboxEvent;
```

Returns the events waiting to be sent, oldest first, up to a limit.

The limit is what makes a push a batch rather than everything a device has
accumulated. §45 assumes slow, expensive links, and a device back from a week
offline should send its backlog in pieces that can each succeed.

```ts
pending(tx: Queryable, limit: number): OutboxEvent[];
```

How many events are still waiting.

```ts
pendingCount(tx: Queryable): number;
```

Marks events the server acknowledged.

Acknowledging an event that is already delivered is not an error: a push
whose response was lost is retried, and the server, being idempotent
(ADR-006), acknowledges it again.

```ts
markDelivered(tx: Queryable, eventIds: readonly string[], at: string): number;
```

Records that an attempt failed, leaving the event pending so it is tried
again.

A transport failure is not the event's fault, and dropping it would lose the
business record it carries.

```ts
markAttemptFailed(tx: Queryable, eventIds: readonly string[], reason: string): number;
```

Marks an event the server refused outright.

This is for a rejection the server will give again however many times it is
retried: a malformed payload, an event type it does not know. Retrying
forever would block everything queued behind it, so it is set aside and
surfaced rather than dropped: the business record it carries still exists on
this device and someone has to decide what to do about it.

```ts
markRejected(tx: Queryable, eventId: string, reason: string): boolean;
```

Returns events the server refused, for someone to look at.

```ts
rejected(tx: Queryable): OutboxEvent[];
```

Removes delivered events older than a cutoff, returning how many went.

Delivered events are kept for a while rather than deleted on acknowledgement,
so a question about what a device sent can still be answered. They are not
kept forever: on cheap hardware an unbounded table is a real problem.

Only delivered events are pruned. Pending ones are the business's data and
rejected ones are waiting for a decision.

```ts
pruneDelivered(tx: Queryable, deliveredBefore: string): number;
```

How many events a single push carries by default.

```ts
const DEFAULT_BATCH_SIZE: 100;
```

Reads a payload as a typed value, for callers that know the event type.

```ts
payloadOf<T>(event: OutboxEvent): T;
```
