## [packages/core/src/data/sync/supplier-applier.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/sync/supplier-applier.ts)

The event type carrying a wholesaler's details.

```ts
const SUPPLIER_SAVED: "SUPPLIER_SAVED";
```

The event type carrying the shop settling a wholesaler's bill.

```ts
const SUPPLIER_PAYMENT: "SUPPLIER_PAYMENT";
```

Applies supplier edits arriving from other devices.

```ts
const supplierApplier: EventApplier;
```

Applies payments to wholesalers arriving from other devices.

Appended, never merged: two devices each settling part of a bill is two
payments, and both stand. A payment that arrives twice (a pull retried after
a lost response, or a device receiving back what it sent) leaves one entry,
because the entry's own identifier came from the device that made it (§27).

```ts
const supplierPaymentApplier: EventApplier;
```

## [packages/core/src/data/sync/transport.ts](../../packages/core/src/data/sync/transport.ts)

How the sync engine reaches the server.

The engine takes this interface rather than calling `fetch` itself, so its
behaviour (what a rejection means, what a lost response means, when to stop)
is testable without a network. That behaviour is where the difficulty is; the
HTTP below it is the easy part.

An event on its way to the server.

```ts
export interface OutgoingEvent {
    readonly event_id: string;
    readonly user_id: string;
    readonly type: string;
    readonly payload: unknown;
    readonly occurred_at: string;
    readonly sequence: number;
}
```

What the server says about one pushed event.

```ts
export interface EventResult {
    readonly event_id: string;
    readonly status?: string;
    readonly reason?: string;
    readonly retry?: boolean;
}
```

The server's answer to a push.

```ts
export interface PushResult {
    readonly results: readonly EventResult[];
    readonly cursor: number;
    readonly accepted: number;
    readonly duplicates: number;
    readonly rejected: number;
}
```

An event arriving from another device.

```ts
export interface IncomingEvent {
    readonly event_id: string;
    readonly device_id: string;
    readonly user_id: string;
    readonly type: string;
    readonly payload: unknown;
    readonly occurred_at: string;
    readonly sequence: number;
}
```

The server's answer to a pull.

```ts
export interface PullResult {
    readonly events: readonly IncomingEvent[];
    readonly cursor: number;
    readonly has_more: boolean;
}
```

The transport the engine drives.

```ts
export interface SyncTransport {
    push(events: readonly OutgoingEvent[]): Promise<PushResult>;
    pull(cursor: number, limit: number): Promise<PullResult>;
}
```

Raised when the server could not be reached or did not answer usefully.

This is deliberately one error rather than a taxonomy. To the engine, a dead
network, a timeout, and a 500 all mean the same thing: nothing was settled,
keep what is queued, try later. §45 assumes this is the normal case, not an
exceptional one.

```ts
class TransportError extends Error {
  /** The HTTP status, when there was one. */
  status: number | undefined;
  constructor(message: string, options?: {
    cause?: unknown;
    status?: number;
});
}
```

What an HTTP transport needs to know.

```ts
export interface HttpTransportOptions {
    readonly baseUrl: string;
    readonly token: () => string;
    readonly fetch?: typeof globalThis.fetch;
    readonly timeoutMs?: number;
}
```

How long a single request may take before it is abandoned.

```ts
const DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS: 30000;
```

A transport that talks to the KOLO API over HTTP.

```ts
class HttpTransport implements SyncTransport {
  constructor(options: HttpTransportOptions);
  push(events: readonly OutgoingEvent[]): Promise<PushResult>;
  pull(cursor: number, limit: number): Promise<PullResult>;
}
```

## [packages/core/src/domain/audit-csv.ts](../../packages/core/src/domain/audit-csv.ts)

The journal as a spreadsheet (spec §39). Pure, so a renderer can assemble an export from pages it was allowed to read;
the client supplies headings and localized cells.

Most entries one export holds; a longer journal is narrowed by period or filter first.

```ts
const AUDIT_EXPORT_LIMIT: 20000;
```

Spreadsheet columns of an exported journal, in order.

```ts
const AUDIT_CSV_COLUMNS: readonly ["date", "time", "utc", "member", "device", "operation", "type", "record", "related", "amount", "details", "source", "status", "id"];
```

```ts
export type AuditCsvColumn = typeof AUDIT_CSV_COLUMNS[number];
```

The client's wording: column headings and the localized cells; everything else is written as stored.

```ts
export interface AuditCsvText {
    readonly headings: Readonly<Record<AuditCsvColumn, string>>;
    readonly operation: (entry: AuditEntry) => string;
    readonly details: (entry: AuditEntry) => string;
    readonly source: (entry: AuditEntry) => string;
    readonly status: (entry: AuditEntry) => string;
    readonly unknown: string;
}
```

The entries as CSV for a spreadsheet: a UTF-8 byte-order mark so accented names open correctly, CRLF lines, and `;`
between cells (what French spreadsheets expect) unless `,` is asked for. Dates and times are this device's local time
beside the UTC instant; amounts are whole XAF as numbers. Text a spreadsheet would run as a formula is escaped.

```ts
auditCsv(entries: readonly AuditEntry[], text: AuditCsvText, delimiter: ";" | "," = ";"): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/domain/auth/login-form.ts](../../packages/core/src/domain/auth/login-form.ts)

The rules of the login form, with no React in them (ADR-004).

A form being filled is transient UI state and belongs in the screen's own
`useState`. What does not belong there is what counts as fillable-in: these
are pure functions from what someone has typed to what the screen shows, so
they can be tested under Node and so a second screen cannot grow a second
answer to the same question.

Nothing here checks a credential. The PIN is verified by the server and
nowhere else (ADR-012); what this prevents is spending a round trip (over a
link §45 assumes is slow and expensive) on something that cannot possibly be
right, such as an empty PIN or a business identifier missing half its digits.

Why a login form cannot be submitted yet.

A code rather than a sentence, for the reason the cart's problems are: the
till speaks French and English (§44), and a message built here would be in
whichever language this file was written in.

```ts
export type LoginProblem = "BUSINESS_REQUIRED" | "BUSINESS_MALFORMED" | "PHONE_REQUIRED" | "PHONE_MALFORMED" | "PIN_REQUIRED" | "PIN_LENGTH" | "PIN_NOT_DIGITS";
```

What someone has typed.

```ts
export interface LoginFormState {
    readonly business: string;
    readonly phone: string;
    readonly pin: string;
}
```

An untouched form.

```ts
const emptyLoginForm: LoginFormState;
```

What the screen shows, and what it would send.

```ts
export interface LoginFormView {
    readonly businessId: string | undefined;
    readonly phone: string;
    readonly problems: readonly LoginProblem[];
}
```

PIN length, matching the server's `auth.ValidatePIN` (4 to 8 digits).

Deliberately *not* matching its weak-PIN rules. Those exist for the moment a
PIN is chosen; applying them at login would tell somebody their PIN is too
simple when what they need to be told is that it is wrong, and would lock
out anyone whose PIN was set before the rule existed.

```ts
const MIN_PIN_LENGTH: 4;
```

```ts
const MAX_PIN_LENGTH: 8;
```

Whether the form can be submitted as it stands.

```ts
canSubmit(view: LoginFormView): boolean;
```

Reads a filled-in form, saying what it would send and what is missing.

```ts
viewLoginForm(form: LoginFormState): LoginFormView;
```

Reads a business identifier as the server will, or undefined if it is not one.

Forgiving about how it was typed and strict about what it is. Every
identifier is a UUID (ADR-011), and the one place a person ever types one is
here: copied from a message, an email or a slip of paper, so it arrives with
stray spaces, in capitals, sometimes with the dashes rubbed out. All of those
name the same shop. Anything else is refused rather than sent, because the
server would refuse it after a round trip on a link that may cost the shop
money (§45).

```ts
normaliseBusinessId(typed: string): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/domain/entry.ts](../../packages/core/src/domain/entry.ts)

Parses whole XAF without coercion, exponent notation, signs or fractional rounding.

```ts
moneyEntry(value: string): number;
```

Parses decimal units to integer thousandths exactly; comma or dot, up to three places.

```ts
quantityEntry(value: string, divisible = true): number;
```

Local calendar-day report range converted to half-open UTC instants, respecting device timezone/DST.

```ts
localDayPeriod(from: string, through: string): { from: string; until: string; };
```

Exact editable decimal quantity; avoids dividing large integer thousandths through a floating-point number.

```ts
quantityInput(thousandths: number): string;
```

Parses a percentage with up to two decimals (comma or dot), 0–100, to basis points exactly: "19,25" is 1925.

```ts
rateEntry(value: string): number;
```

Exact editable percentage for basis points: 1925 is "19.25", 1000 is "10".

```ts
rateInput(basisPoints: number): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/domain/money.ts](../../packages/core/src/domain/money.ts)

Turning amounts into the text a shopkeeper reads (§46).

Everything in this system is XAF, and **XAF has no subunit**: there is no
centime, and the smallest coin is five francs. So a "minor unit" here is a
whole franc, and formatting is grouping digits rather than placing a decimal
point. A currency with cents would need a different function; writing one
that pretended to handle both is how a franc becomes a centime somewhere.

## Why the grouping is done by hand

`Intl.NumberFormat` would do this, and on a desktop browser it would be the
right answer. On the cheap Android hardware this targets, the JavaScript
engine may ship without full ICU data, and the failure mode is not a crash:
it is a number that groups differently on one device than another. Prices
that read differently on the owner's phone and the till are worse than plain
ones, so the separator is applied here and is the same everywhere.

Parsing is here too, and is the more dangerous direction: what a cashier
types becomes money that gets written down.

The languages the app speaks (§46).

```ts
export type Language = "fr" | "en";
```

What the currency is called on a receipt. Locally it is FCFA, not XAF.

```ts
const CURRENCY_LABEL: "FCFA";
```

Formats a whole-franc amount, without the currency name.

Negative amounts keep their sign: a drawer that is short, or a customer in
credit, is a real number and rounding it to nothing would hide the one figure
worth looking at.

```ts
formatAmount(minor: number, language: Language = "fr"): string;
```

Formats an amount with the currency, as it appears on a receipt.

The name follows the number in both languages, which is what people here
write: "12 500 FCFA". The space before it is a no-break space, so a price
never breaks across two lines with the currency stranded on the second.

```ts
formatMoney(minor: number, language: Language = "fr"): string;
```

Formats a quantity held in thousandths.

Whole amounts print whole: "3 sacs", never "3.000 sacs". A shop selling by
the sack should never see a decimal point, and a shop selling by the kilo
needs one only when there is a fraction to show. Trailing zeroes are dropped
for the same reason.

```ts
formatQuantity(thousandths: number, language: Language = "fr"): string;
```

The plural of a shop-entered unit name, in the interface language. Symbols, abbreviations
(two letters or fewer, all capitals, a full stop or a digit) and words already ending in
s/x/z are kept. Only the first word changes, so "sac de 50 kg" becomes "sacs de 50 kg".
French: -au/-eau/-eu take x, -al becomes -aux. English: consonant + y becomes -ies,
-ch/-sh/-s/-x/-z take es.

```ts
pluralUnit(unit: string, language: Language = "fr"): string;
```

A quantity followed by its unit, singular or plural by the language's own rules.

```ts
formatQuantityWithUnit(thousandths: number, unit: string, language: Language = "fr"): string;
```

What went wrong with something a cashier typed.

```ts
export type ParseFailure = "EMPTY" | "NOT_A_NUMBER" | "NEGATIVE" | "TOO_LARGE" | "FRACTIONAL";
```

Either the amount, or why it could not be read.

```ts
export type ParsedAmount = {
    readonly ok: true;
    readonly minor: number;
} | {
    readonly ok: false;
    readonly failure: ParseFailure;
};
```

Reads an amount a cashier typed into whole francs.

Deliberately strict, because this is the direction that writes money down.
It accepts the separators a person actually types (spaces, and the comma or
dot they use for grouping) and refuses anything else rather than guessing.

A fraction is refused rather than rounded. There is no half franc, and a till
that silently rounded 100.5 would be deciding on the shopkeeper's behalf
which way their money went.

```ts
parseAmount(text: string): ParsedAmount;
```

Reads a quantity a cashier typed into thousandths.

Fractions are allowed here and refused above, and the difference is the
point: half a kilo is an ordinary thing to sell, and half a franc does not
exist. Beyond three decimal places is refused rather than rounded, because
that is the resolution the ledger stores and a quietly rounded quantity would
not match what was weighed.

```ts
parseQuantity(text: string): ParsedAmount;
```

A tax rate in basis points as people write it: "19,25 %" in French (narrow no-break space before the sign),
"19.25%" in English. Trailing zeroes are dropped, so 1000 reads "10 %".

```ts
formatRate(basisPoints: number, language: Language = "fr"): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/domain/pos/cart.ts](../../packages/core/src/domain/pos/cart.ts)

A cart being filled at the till.

```ts
export interface CartState {
    readonly lines: readonly CartLine[];
    readonly payments: readonly CartPayment[];
    readonly customerId?: string | undefined;
    readonly discountMinor: number;
    readonly note?: string | undefined;
}
```

An empty till.

```ts
const emptyCart: CartState;
```

Why a cart cannot be completed yet.

A code rather than a sentence, because the till speaks French and English
(§46) and a message built here would be in whichever one this file was
written in. The screen turns these into words.

```ts
export type Problem = "EMPTY" | "OVERPAID" | "DISCOUNT_EXCEEDS_SALE" | "CREDIT_NEEDS_CUSTOMER" | "LINE_DISCOUNT_EXCEEDS_LINE" | "INVALID_AMOUNT" | "TOO_LARGE";
```

What the till shows while the cart is being filled.

```ts
export interface CartView {
    readonly subtotalMinor: number;
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly paidMinor: number;
    readonly outstandingMinor: number;
    readonly itemsThousandths: number;
    readonly problems: readonly Problem[];
}
```

Whether the cart can be completed as it stands.

```ts
isComplete(view: CartView): boolean;
```

Adds a product to the cart, or adds to the line already holding it.

Merging rather than appending, because scanning the same tin four times is
one line of four in every shop anyone has stood in. A cashier who genuinely
wants two lines at different prices changes the price on one, which
 makes a different line.

The price and name are copied from the product **now**, and stay copied: a
receipt reprinted next year has to show what was charged, and the product
will have been repriced by then (§41). The cost copied here is only a
preview: completing the sale freezes the cost from the shared costing policy
(§8), so the editable purchase price never decides a sale's profit.

```ts
addProduct(state: CartState, product: Product, ids: {
    readonly lineId: string;
    readonly movementId: string;
}, quantity = QUANTITY_SCALE): CartState;
```

Sets a line's quantity, removing it at zero or below.

Removing rather than keeping a zero line: a cashier holding the minus key
means to take the thing off, and a line of nothing on a receipt is a puzzle.

```ts
setQuantity(state: CartState, lineId: string, quantity: number): CartState;
```

Overrides what one line charges, which §41 records as a price change.

```ts
setLinePrice(state: CartState, lineId: string, unitPriceMinor: number): CartState;
```

Takes an amount off one line.

```ts
setLineDiscount(state: CartState, lineId: string, discountMinor: number): CartState;
```

```ts
removeLine(state: CartState, lineId: string): CartState;
```

Takes an amount off the whole sale, after the lines.

```ts
setDiscount(state: CartState, discountMinor: number): CartState;
```

Tenders a payment against the cart.

A sale has *many* payments (split cash and Mobile Money is an ordinary case
(§11), so these append rather than replace. Each names an account, never a
method, which is what keeps a provider a row rather than a release (§10).

```ts
addPayment(state: CartState, payment: CartPayment): CartState;
```

```ts
removePayment(state: CartState, paymentId: string): CartState;
```

Names who the sale is for, or nobody.

```ts
setCustomer(state: CartState, customerId?: string): CartState;
```

```ts
setNote(state: CartState, note?: string): CartState;
```

What the till shows, and everything wrong with the cart so far.

Never throws. The arithmetic comes from `subtotalOf`, the data layer's own,
so the number on the screen and the number on the receipt are the same
number by construction rather than by agreement.

```ts
view(state: CartState): CartView;
```

Turns a filled cart into the thing `completeSale` takes.

The identifiers are supplied rather than drawn here, because a screen that
re-rendered would otherwise draw new ones and a retried tap would write a
second sale. Drawing them once, where the tap is handled, is what makes the
write idempotent (§27, ADR-011).

The credit entry identifier is required exactly when the cart leaves
something owing, which is the rule `completeSale` enforces and the server
enforces again. Deciding it here means a cashier is told at the till rather
than after the sale is on the wire.

```ts
toCart(state: CartState, identity: {
    readonly saleId: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly branchId?: string | undefined;
    readonly number: string;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
    readonly recordedAt: string;
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly creditTransactionId: string;
}): Cart;
```

## [packages/core/src/domain/receipt-number.ts](../../packages/core/src/domain/receipt-number.ts)

Short display form of an offline-unique sale number. Desktop numbers are `V-` plus the sale
UUID; mobile numbers are `V-YYYYMMDD-` plus the UUID's last twelve hex digits. Both end in
the sale ID's last eight hex digits, so a sale shows the same `V-XXXXXXXX` on every device.
Other numbers are returned unchanged; receipts and printouts keep the stored number whole.

```ts
shortReceiptNumber(number: string): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/domain/report-document.ts](../../packages/core/src/domain/report-document.ts)

A report as a printable A4 page (spec §39). Pure, so each client turns the report it was allowed to read into the
same document: desktop saves it as PDF, mobile hands it to the system print dialog (which also saves PDF).

Labels the document needs, already in the device language; business names stay as stored. `uncostedLines` and
`uncostedProducts` follow a count ("3 lines without a cost"); `truncated` is shown when a list was cut at the row
limit (totals above it stay exact).

```ts
const REPORT_DOCUMENT_LABELS: readonly ["name", "count", "total", "salesTotal", "transactions", "itemsSold", "revenue", "grossProfit", "expenses", "netResult", "customerDebt", "supplierDebt", "lowStock", "uncostedLines", "uncostedProducts", "purchasesTotal", "deliveries", "noSupplier", "uncategorized", "account", "moneyIn", "moneyOut", "net", "closing", "stockValue", "stock", "unitCost", "value", "negativeStock", "taxRate", "taxGross", "taxAmount", "taxNet", "taxNote", "noData", "truncated"];
```

One wording per  entry.

```ts
export type ReportDocumentLabels = Readonly<Record<typeof REPORT_DOCUMENT_LABELS[number], string>>;
```

What surrounds the figures: who, which report, which period, when it was produced.

```ts
export interface ReportDocumentText {
    readonly language: Language;
    readonly shop: string;
    readonly title: string;
    readonly period: string;
    readonly generated: string;
    readonly credit: string;
    readonly labels: ReportDocumentLabels;
}
```

One table cell: its text, whether it is a figure (right-aligned) and whether it is below zero.

```ts
export interface ReportCell {
    readonly text: string;
    readonly numeric?: boolean;
    readonly negative?: boolean;
}
```

A table's headings and rows.

```ts
export interface ReportTable {
    readonly columns: readonly ReportCell[];
    readonly rows: readonly (readonly ReportCell[])[];
}
```

The figures of a report, ready for any layout: headline tiles, notes and at most one table.

```ts
export interface ReportSheet {
    readonly tiles: readonly (readonly [
        string,
        string,
        boolean?
    ])[];
    readonly notes: readonly string[];
    readonly table?: ReportTable;
    readonly empty?: string;
    readonly truncated: boolean;
}
```

The figures of a report under the client's labels; nothing here reads the database.

```ts
reportSheet(value: ReportResult, labels: ReportDocumentLabels, language: Language): ReportSheet;
```

The report as a self-contained A4 HTML page: no script, no external resource, every value escaped. Table headings
repeat on each printed page and rows never split across pages.

```ts
reportHtml(value: ReportResult, text: ReportDocumentText): string;
```

## [packages/core/src/exchange/types.ts](../../packages/core/src/exchange/types.ts)

Native crypto implementations sign UTF-8 canonical bytes with protected Ed25519 keys.

```ts
export interface ExchangeCrypto {
    sha256(message: string): Promise<string>;
    verify(publicKey: string, message: string, signatureHex: string): Promise<boolean>;
}
```

Stable exchange failures; never includes payloads, private keys or native error text.

```ts
class ExchangeError extends Error {
  code: "invalid_event" | "invalid_payload" | "untrusted_device" | "invalid_signature" | "conflict" | "return_conflict" | "scope_mismatch" | "permission_denied" | "unsupported_event" | "missing_dependency" | "authority_revision" | "device_revoked" | "invalid_receipt" | "invalid_destination" | "fingerprint_mismatch" | "locked";
  constructor(code: ExchangeError["code"]);
}
```

Durable effect outcomes; deferred/unsupported/quarantined records produce no business effects.

```ts
export type EventOutcome = "applied" | "duplicate" | "deferred" | "quarantined" | "unsupported";
```

An opaque verified capability; matching fields on a forged object confer no authority.

```ts
export interface VerifiedEvent {
    readonly eventId: string;
    readonly digest: string;
}
```

## [packages/core/src/identity/credentials.ts](../../packages/core/src/identity/credentials.ts)

Native/OpenSSL PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 policy; never implemented with a JS digest.

```ts
const CREDENTIAL_ITERATIONS: 600000;
```

Platform crypto primitives. Derivation is asynchronous; private keys never enter SQLite.

```ts
export interface CredentialCrypto {
    randomHex(bytes: number): string;
    derive(secret: string, saltHex: string, iterations: number): Promise<string>;
    equalHex(left: string, right: string): boolean;
    sign?(privateKey: string, message: string): Promise<string>;
    deviceKey(): Promise<{
        readonly publicKey: string;
        readonly privateKey: string;
    }>;
    encryptionKeyPair?(): Promise<{
        readonly publicKey: string;
        readonly privateKey: string;
    }>;
    agree?(privateKey: string, peerPublicKey: string): Promise<string>;
}
```

Accepts 6–8 digit PINs or passwords of 12–256 characters; native storage is mandatory.

```ts
validateLocalSecret(secret: string): void;
```

Salted expensive verifier + Ed25519 private key, held entirely in a protected
platform SecretStore. SQLite receives only public key and opaque references.
Native writes are serialized; provision retries never replace an existing
identity/credential. A 256-bit recovery code independently resets the owner PIN.
Adapter instances own no database/network and expose no private-key read API.

```ts
protectedCredentials(store: SecretStore, crypto: CredentialCrypto): CredentialPort;
```

## [packages/core/src/identity/device-reset.ts](../../packages/core/src/identity/device-reset.ts)

Every protected-store key a shop database refers to on this device: each member's local
credential, the installation key and its manifest, and a pending setup's installation manifest.
A device reset forgets these before deleting the database files, so no secret outlives its shop.
References that belong to another device (`remote.*`, after a restore or pairing) are skipped.

```ts
localSecretKeys(tx: Queryable): string[];
```

## [packages/core/src/identity/members.ts](../../packages/core/src/identity/members.ts)

Local staff details, resolved from current memberships instead of cloud role metadata.

```ts
export interface LocalMember {
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly role: Role;
    readonly active: boolean;
    readonly revision: number;
    readonly customRoleId?: string;
}
```

Owner-only staff creation with durable intent around asynchronous native provisioning.

```ts
createLocalMember(context: OperationContext, input: {
    readonly name: string;
    readonly role: Exclude<Role, "OWNER">;
    readonly secret: string;
}): Promise<string>;
```

Owner resets a staff credential without changing its user ID, role, grants or attribution.

```ts
resetLocalMemberCredential(context: OperationContext, userId: string, secret: string): Promise<void>;
```

Owner unlock resumes fully durable staff provisioning after a crash; incomplete intent remains retryable.

```ts
resumeMemberProvisioning(context: OperationContext): Promise<void>;
```

Lists current staff under users.view, without credential references or secrets.

```ts
listLocalMembers(context: OperationContext): readonly LocalMember[];
```

## [packages/core/src/identity/recovery.ts](../../packages/core/src/identity/recovery.ts)

Reveals an owner's protected recovery code after both active-context and PIN proof.

```ts
ownerRecoveryCode(context: OperationContext, secret: string): Promise<string>;
```

Restores owner access using a separate 256-bit recovery code, never a shop UUID
or expired cloud token. The credential-change intent contains references only.
Native failure consumes a durable throttled attempt. Native success is followed
by atomic credential-reference/authority/audit update; boot can complete the gap.
Ten wrong recovery proofs require external authenticated backup recovery (F3).

```ts
recoverOwnerAccess(input: {
    readonly recoveryCode: string;
    readonly newSecret: string;
}, ports: WorkspacePorts): Promise<void>;
```

Completes a durable native recovery write after restart, without PIN/recovery code in SQLite.

```ts
resumeOwnerRecovery(ports: WorkspacePorts): Promise<void>;
```

Owner-only actor suspension/reinstatement; owner cannot suspend themselves.

```ts
setMemberActive(context: OperationContext, userId: string, active: boolean): void;
```

## [packages/core/src/identity/roles.ts](../../packages/core/src/identity/roles.ts)

One staff role's permissions as the shop resolves them now (ADR-037).

```ts
export interface RoleView {
    readonly role: StaffRole;
    readonly permissions: readonly Permission[];
    readonly customized: boolean;
    readonly revision: number;
}
```

The role matrix under users.view: every staff role with its current permissions. The owner always holds all.

```ts
listRoles(context: OperationContext): readonly RoleView[];
```

Owner-only: sets the permissions every member with this role starts from (ADR-037), recorded as a signed
`ROLE_PERMISSIONS_SET` that other tills apply. `null`, or a set equal to the built-in defaults, returns the role to
those defaults. Members' own grants and revocations still apply on top. Asking for the current set changes nothing.

```ts
setRolePermissions(context: OperationContext, role: StaffRole, permissions: readonly Permission[] | null): void;
```

A shop-defined role: a named copy of a built-in staff role with its own permissions.

```ts
export interface CustomRoleView {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly baseRole: StaffRole;
    readonly permissions: readonly Permission[];
    readonly deleted: boolean;
    readonly holders: number;
    readonly revision: number;
}
```

Shop-defined roles under users.view, by name: every live role, and deleted ones that members still hold.

```ts
listCustomRoles(context: OperationContext): readonly CustomRoleView[];
```

Owner-only: a new role named `name`, starting from the shop's current permissions for `baseRole`. `id` is the
caller's request ID: repeating the same creation returns the stored role. Names are unique, ignoring case.

```ts
createCustomRole(context: OperationContext, input: {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly name: string;
    readonly baseRole: StaffRole;
}): CustomRoleView;
```

Owner-only: renames a live custom role or replaces its permissions; it reaches every holder's next command.

```ts
updateCustomRole(context: OperationContext, id: string, change: {
    readonly name?: string;
    readonly permissions?: readonly Permission[];
}): CustomRoleView;
```

Owner-only: deletes a custom role nobody holds (`role_in_use` otherwise). Deleting a deleted role changes nothing.

```ts
deleteCustomRole(context: OperationContext, id: string): void;
```

Owner-only: moves a staff member to another staff role or to a live custom role, keeping their own grants and
revocations. The owner's role never changes and no member becomes owner. Asking for the current role changes nothing.

```ts
changeMemberRole(context: OperationContext, userId: string, role: StaffRole | {
    readonly customRoleId: string;
}): void;
```

## [packages/core/src/identity/types.ts](../../packages/core/src/identity/types.ts)

Stable failures for local setup, authentication and application authorization.

```ts
export type WorkspaceErrorCode = "invalid_input" | "already_configured" | "legacy_installation" | "setup_pending" | "not_configured" | "credential_unavailable" | "invalid_credential" | "retry_later" | "recovery_required" | "locked" | "actor_inactive" | "permission_denied" | "scope_mismatch" | "role_in_use" | "duplicate_name";
```

Contains a stable code and optional retry time, never a PIN or native error.

```ts
class WorkspaceError extends Error {
  code: WorkspaceErrorCode;
  retryAt: string | undefined;
  constructor(code: WorkspaceErrorCode, retryAt?: string);
}
```

Public key and opaque native references; no private key or verifier bytes.

```ts
export interface ProvisionedIdentity {
    readonly publicKey: string;
    readonly keyReference: string;
    readonly credentialReference: string;
}
```

Native credential boundary, asynchronous and independent of SQLite/network.
Implementations must use a reviewed salted expensive KDF and protected native
storage. Provisioning is idempotent by installation ID: existing material must
be verified against the request, never silently overwritten. Inspect returns
material only after BOTH credential and device key are durably provisioned.
Lost/incomplete material returns undefined; exceptions must not expose secrets.
Native adapters and their performance/security gates are documented separately.

```ts
export interface CredentialPort {
    provision(request: {
        readonly installationId: string;
        readonly businessId: string;
        readonly userId: string;
        readonly deviceId: string;
        readonly secret: string;
    }): Promise<ProvisionedIdentity>;
    inspect(installationId: string): Promise<ProvisionedIdentity | undefined>;
    verify(credentialReference: string, secret: string): Promise<boolean>;
    sign?(keyReference: string, message: string): Promise<string>;
    recover?(oldReference: string, newReference: string, recoveryCode: string, newSecret: string): Promise<void>;
    hasCredential?(reference: string): Promise<boolean>;
    recoveryCode?(reference: string, secret: string): Promise<string | undefined>;
    encryptionKey?(keyReference: string): Promise<string>;
    agree?(keyReference: string, peerPublicKey: string): Promise<string>;
}
```

Injected local dependencies; IDs must be fresh UUIDv7 and clock ISO UTC.

```ts
export interface WorkspacePorts {
    readonly database: Database;
    readonly credentials: CredentialPort;
    readonly newId: () => string;
    readonly now: () => string;
}
```

Offline setup request. Credentials are passed only to the native port.

```ts
export interface CreateWorkspaceInput {
    readonly businessName: string;
    readonly currency: "XAF";
    readonly ownerName: string;
    readonly secret: string;
    readonly deviceLabel: string;
    readonly platform: "ANDROID" | "IOS" | "DESKTOP";
    readonly accounts?: readonly {
        readonly name: string;
        readonly kind: AccountKind;
    }[];
}
```

Persisted public installation metadata, independent of optional cloud sessions.

```ts
export interface Installation {
    readonly installationId: string;
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly businessName: string;
    readonly ownerId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
    readonly publicKey: string;
    readonly configuredAt: string;
}
```

Boot states are explicit: legacy data is never auto-adopted or relabeled.

```ts
export type InstallationState = {
    readonly state: "empty" | "legacy";
} | {
    readonly state: "pending";
    readonly installationId: string;
} | {
    readonly state: "locked" | "unlocked";
    readonly installation: Installation;
};
```

An opaque, in-memory unlock capability. Commands accept only the exact object
issued by unlockWorkspace; copied/forged IDs or claimed roles confer no access.
Lock, replacement unlock and restart invalidate it. No cloud expiry applies.

```ts
export interface OperationContext {
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly deviceId: string;
}
```

## [packages/core/src/identity/workspace.ts](../../packages/core/src/identity/workspace.ts)

Reads boot status without credentials, remote enrollment or side effects.

```ts
currentInstallation(database: Database): InstallationState;
```

Reserves a durable setup intent, provisions native secrets, then atomically
commits shop/owner/device/accounts/membership/bootstrap audit and completion.
Empty installations only; existing demo/server shops require explicit migration.
Failure retains intent for retry/recovery without recording raw secrets. Retrying
requires the same public setup fields and reuses ALL previously allocated IDs.
Returns configured/locked metadata; unlocking is a separate proof of credential.

```ts
createWorkspace(input: CreateWorkspaceInput, ports: WorkspacePorts): Promise<Installation>;
```

Recovers the secret/SQLite crash gap without a password or new IDs. Completes
only when the trusted native port confirms fully durable provisioning. Missing
material leaves pending setup intact for a same-input createWorkspace retry.

```ts
recoverWorkspaceSetup(ports: WorkspacePorts): Promise<InstallationState>;
```

Retries an existing fresh/owner-proven legacy provisioning intent without new IDs or renamed data.

```ts
resumeWorkspaceProvisioning(secret: string, ports: WorkspacePorts): Promise<Installation>;
```

UI display metadata resolved under current local authority; commands still recheck on every use.

```ts
unlockedActor(context: OperationContext): { readonly businessName: string; readonly userName: string; readonly branchId: string; readonly isOwner: boolean; };
```

Explicit owner-authenticated conversion of a legacy server/demo installation.
The platform authority transport must prove the same shop and existing device,
an active OWNER and a fresh session. All existing shop/device/entity/event IDs,
financial facts, cloud session and outbox remain untouched. Cached roles/tokens
alone are never owner proof. Native PIN provisioning uses recoverable setup intent.

```ts
adoptLegacyWorkspace(input: {
    readonly phone: string;
    readonly serverPin: string;
    readonly localSecret: string;
}, ports: WorkspacePorts, authority: AuthTransport): Promise<Installation>;
```

Proves an active local actor's credential and issues an opaque local context.
Retry reservation is durable BEFORE async native verification: concurrent attempts
cannot bypass it. Failures delay 1/2/4/.../60 seconds and ten require owner recovery
through separate recovery proof. Restart does not clear retries. Native errors consume an
attempt; no secret/error details escape. Membership is rechecked after verification.

```ts
unlockWorkspace(input: {
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly secret: string;
}, ports: WorkspacePorts): Promise<OperationContext>;
```

Host-protected reference that lets a trusted host reopen this member's session after a restart
without a secret. It names the member's current credential, so a code reset, owner recovery or
restored backup makes it stale. Hosts must keep it in OS-protected storage only.

```ts
export interface RememberedMember {
    readonly businessId: string;
    readonly userId: string;
    readonly credentialReference: string;
}
```

Returns the remembered-session reference for an unlocked context's member.

```ts
rememberMember(context: OperationContext): RememberedMember;
```

Reopens a remembered member without secret proof. The member, device and business must still be
active, not locked out, and hold the same credential; any mismatch throws and grants nothing.

```ts
resumeWorkspace(remembered: RememberedMember, ports: WorkspacePorts): OperationContext;
```

Invalidates the active capability and in-flight unlocks without altering records/cloud sessions.

```ts
lockWorkspace(database: Database): void;
```

Owner-only permission grant, with authority revision and immutable local event/audit.

```ts
grantPermission(context: OperationContext, userId: string, permission: Permission): void;
```

Owner-only revocation; takes effect on the target's next local command without re-unlock.

```ts
revokePermission(context: OperationContext, userId: string, permission: Permission): void;
```

Owner-only: drops the member's own grant or revocation of one permission, so it follows their role again (ADR-037).

```ts
clearPermissionOverride(context: OperationContext, userId: string, permission: Permission): void;
```

## [packages/core/src/rules/amounts.ts](../../packages/core/src/rules/amounts.ts)

Pure exact arithmetic shared by clients and server. Money is integer XAF.

```ts
const QUANTITY_SCALE: 1000;
```

Stable rule failures, with no platform/database dependency.

```ts
class RuleError extends Error {
  code: "invalid_amount" | "overflow" | "discount" | "overpaid" | "counterparty";
  constructor(code: RuleError["code"], message: string = code);
}
```

Checks the number boundary before conversion to exact BigInt intermediates.

```ts
integer(value: number): bigint;
```

Converts only exact safe integer results; never rounds an unsafe number boundary.

```ts
amount(value: bigint): number;
```

Exact multiply/divide, rounded half away from zero, including negative corrections.

```ts
multiplyRatio(value: number, numerator: number, denominator: number): number;
```

Sums signed safe inputs using exact intermediates and checks the final result.

```ts
sumAmounts(values: readonly number[]): number;
```

Credit policy compares exact prospective debt, including an existing credit balance.

```ts
exceedsCreditLimit(outstandingMinor: number, furtherMinor: number, limitMinor: number | undefined): boolean;
```

## [packages/core/src/rules/customer-settlement.ts](../../packages/core/src/rules/customer-settlement.ts)

How an amount posted to a customer's account uses prepaid credit and adds debt.

```ts
export interface CustomerSettlement {
    readonly balanceBeforeMinor: number;
    readonly balanceAfterMinor: number;
    readonly creditAppliedMinor: number;
    readonly debtAddedMinor: number;
}
```

Positive balances are owed by the customer; negative balances are credit held by the shop.

```ts
customerSettlement(balanceBeforeMinor: number, onAccountMinor: number): CustomerSettlement;
```

The change a named customer can leave on their account: cash handed over beyond the cash due, on a sale with nothing
left owing. Zero when there is nothing to keep: no customer, nothing tendered or too little, or a sale left on account.

```ts
keepableChange(input: {
    readonly hasCustomer: boolean;
    readonly cashDueMinor: number;
    readonly tenderedMinor: number | undefined;
    readonly outstandingMinor: number;
}): number;
```

## [packages/core/src/rules/pricing.ts](../../packages/core/src/rules/pricing.ts)

Minimal pricing facts; no SQL/framework/actor IDs enter a pure calculation.

```ts
export interface PricedLine {
    readonly unitPriceMinor: number;
    readonly quantity: number;
    readonly discountMinor?: number | undefined;
}
```

A sale's arithmetic, independently usable by the till, importer and server.

```ts
export interface SaleTotals {
    readonly subtotalMinor: number;
    readonly totalMinor: number;
    readonly paidMinor: number;
    readonly outstandingMinor: number;
}
```

Exact line price in XAF, with positive thousandths and bounded nonnegative discount.

```ts
lineTotal(line: PricedLine, index = 0): number;
```

Sum of independently rounded lines; aggregate overflow is rejected.

```ts
subtotalOf(lines: readonly PricedLine[]): number;
```

Paid amounts are recorded allocations, not cash tendered or change.

```ts
settlement(totalMinor: number, payments: readonly {
    readonly amountMinor: number;
}[], counterpartyId?: string): { readonly paidMinor: number; readonly outstandingMinor: number; };
```

Exact sale subtotal/discount/settlement; callers retain persistence validation.

```ts
saleTotals(input: {
    readonly lines: readonly PricedLine[];
    readonly payments: readonly {
        readonly amountMinor: number;
    }[];
    readonly discountMinor?: number | undefined;
    readonly customerId?: string | undefined;
}): SaleTotals;
```

Exact delivery pricing using the same line rounding and settlement as sales.

```ts
purchaseTotals(input: {
    readonly lines: readonly {
        readonly unitCostMinor: number;
        readonly quantity: number;
    }[];
    readonly payments: readonly {
        readonly amountMinor: number;
    }[];
    readonly supplierId?: string | undefined;
}): Omit<SaleTotals, "subtotalMinor">;
```

## [packages/core/src/rules/returns.ts](../../packages/core/src/rules/returns.ts)

Historical line pricing and IDs used to allocate a sale's whole-bill discount deterministically.

```ts
export interface ReturnPriceLine {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly unitPriceMinor: number;
    readonly quantity: number;
    readonly discountMinor?: number | undefined;
}
```

Allocates whole-bill discount by largest remainder, ties by line ID; exact XAF totals are preserved.

```ts
discountedLineTotals(lines: readonly ReturnPriceLine[], discountMinor: number): ReadonlyMap<string, number>;
```

Partial refund equals the change in cumulative rounded entitlement; the final return pays every remaining franc.

```ts
incrementalReturnMinor(originalNetMinor: number, originalQuantity: number, alreadyReturned: number, returning: number): number;
```

## [packages/core/src/rules/tax.ts](../../packages/core/src/rules/tax.ts)

Tax rates are basis points: 1925 is 19.25 %, 10000 is 100 %.

```ts
const TAX_RATE_SCALE: 10000;
```

Checks a rate before it reaches arithmetic or storage.

```ts
checkTaxRate(rateBasisPoints: number): number;
```

The tax contained in a tax-inclusive amount (ADR-033): `amount × rate / (10000 + rate)`, exact and rounded half away
from zero. Signed, so a period whose returns exceed its sales yields negative tax.

```ts
includedTax(amountMinor: number, rateBasisPoints: number): number;
```

Tax-inclusive amounts at one rate: what the customer paid, the tax inside it and the amount before tax.

```ts
export interface TaxGroup {
    readonly rateBasisPoints: number;
    readonly grossMinor: number;
    readonly taxMinor: number;
    readonly netMinor: number;
}
```

Groups signed tax-inclusive amounts by rate and computes each group's tax once, so rounding happens per rate rather
than per line. Groups are ordered by rate, highest first; a group whose amounts cancel to zero is kept.

```ts
taxGroups(amounts: readonly {
    readonly rateBasisPoints: number;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
}[]): readonly TaxGroup[];
```

A sale line with the rate frozen on it at the time of sale.

```ts
export interface TaxedLine extends ReturnPriceLine {
    readonly taxRateBasisPoints: number;
}
```

A sale's tax by rate. Each line is reduced by its own discount and its deterministic share of the bill discount (the
allocation returns use), so the groups' gross amounts add up exactly to the sale total.

```ts
saleTaxGroups(lines: readonly TaxedLine[], saleDiscountMinor: number): readonly TaxGroup[];
```

## [packages/core/src/rules/terms.ts](../../packages/core/src/rules/terms.ts)

Terms applied when a shop has stored none (ADR-033).

```ts
const DEFAULT_TERMS_DAYS: 30;
```

How far ahead an amount counts as "due soon".

```ts
const DUE_SOON_DAYS: 3;
```

Checks payment terms in whole days, 0 (due at once) to 3650.

```ts
checkTermsDays(days: number): number;
```

When a debt that occurred at `occurredAt` falls due under `days` of terms (UTC instant).

```ts
dueAt(occurredAt: string, days: number): string;
```

One signed ledger entry: positive adds to what is owed, negative reduces it.

```ts
export interface LedgerEntry {
    readonly id: string;
    readonly amountMinor: number;
    readonly occurredAt: string;
}
```

What is owed and when it falls due. Amounts are XAF; times are ISO instants.

```ts
export interface DebtAging {
    readonly balanceMinor: number;
    readonly overdueMinor: number;
    readonly dueSoonMinor: number;
    readonly oldestDueAt?: string | undefined;
    readonly nextDueAt?: string | undefined;
    readonly lastPassedDueAt?: string | undefined;
}
```

Ages an outstanding balance (ADR-033). Payments and other reductions settle the oldest debt first, so what remains
is exactly the newest debts that add up to the balance; the oldest of those may be partly settled. `debts` are the
positive entries in any order. A balance of zero or less (paid up, or in credit) owes nothing.

```ts
ageOutstanding(debts: readonly LedgerEntry[], balanceMinor: number, termsDays: number, now: string, soonDays = DUE_SOON_DAYS): DebtAging;
```

Ages a whole signed ledger: its sum is the balance and its positive entries are the debts.

```ts
ageLedger(entries: readonly LedgerEntry[], termsDays: number, now: string, soonDays = DUE_SOON_DAYS): DebtAging;
```
